\displaystyle\prod_{i\in[k,l]}^{<}{\bm{\mathrm{t}}}_{i}=\prod_{k\leq i\leq l}{{\bm{\mathrm{t}}}}_{i}:={{\bm{\mathrm{t}}}}_{k}{{\bm{\mathrm{t}}}}_{k+1}\cdots{{\bm{\mathrm{t}}}}_{l},\quad\prod_{i\in[k,l]}^{>}{\bm{\mathrm{t}}}_{i}=\prod_{l\geq i\geq k}{{\bm{\mathrm{t}}}}_{i}:={{\bm{\mathrm{t}}}}_{l}{{\bm{\mathrm{t}}}}_{l-1}\cdots{{\bm{\mathrm{t}}}}_{k}.

For
1\leq k\leq n,1\leq h\leq n-1
,
p>0
,
\lambda\in\Lambda(n,r)
and
A\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N})
, let

(7.0.2)

\begin{aligned} E^{\star}_{h,\lambda,p}&:=(\lambda+pE_{h,h+1}-pE_{h+1,h+1}|O),\\
F^{\star}_{h,\lambda,p}&:=(\lambda+pE_{h+1,h}-pE_{h,h}|O),\end{aligned}\qquad\;\begin{aligned} A^{+,p}_{h,k}&:=A+pE_{h,k}-pE_{h+1,k},\\
A^{-,p}_{h,k}&:=A-pE_{h,k}+pE_{h+1,k}.\end{aligned}

Then, in the notations in (
4.0.3
) and (
2.2.2
), we actually have

\displaystyle E^{\star}_{h,\lambda}=E^{\star}_{h,\lambda,1},\quad\;F^{\star}_{h,\lambda}=F^{\star}_{h,\lambda,1},\quad\;A^{+}_{h,k}=A^{+,1}_{h,k},\quad\;A^{-}_{h,k}=A^{-,1}_{h,k}.

Thus,
A^{+,p}_{h,k}
(resp.,
A^{-,p}_{h,k}
) is a
p
-up
(resp.,
p
-down
) matrix.

The multiplication formulas given in Lemma
4.1
are “order 1” standard multiplication formulas. The following lemma can be used to derive the higher order ones.

Lemma 7.1
.

Fix
1\leq h\leq n-1
and
p>0
. For any
\lambda\in\Lambda(n,r)
, we have

(7.1.1)

\displaystyle[E^{\star}_{h,\lambda,p}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{[p]^{!}}\prod_{p>i\geq 0}[E^{\star}_{h,{\lambda}+i{\alpha}_{h}}]=\frac{1}{[p]}[E^{\star}_{h,{\lambda}+(p-1){\alpha}_{h},1}][E^{\star}_{h,{\lambda},p-1}]\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\text{in the case }\lambda_{h+1}\geq p)},

(7.1.2)

\displaystyle[F^{\star}_{h,\lambda,p}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{[p]^{!}}\prod_{p>i\geq 0}[F^{\star}_{h,{\lambda}+i{\alpha}_{h}}]=\frac{1}{[p]}[F^{\star}_{h,{\lambda}+(p-1){\alpha}_{h},1}][F^{\star}_{h,{\lambda},p-1}]\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\text{in the case }\lambda_{h}\geq p)}.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.1
(2), one checks easily that, for any
1\leq j\leq p
,

\displaystyle{[\lambda+j\alpha_{h}-E_{h+1,h+1}+E_{h,h+1}|O]}\cdot

\displaystyle[j]![\lambda-jE_{h+1,h+1}+jE_{h,h+1}|O]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[j+1]![\lambda-(j+1)E_{h+1,h+1}+(j+1)E_{h,h+1}|O].

Thus, (
7.1.1
) follows from induction. (
7.1.2
) can be proved similarly by Lemma
4.1
(3).
∎

The following facts can be easily checked by the definition of the preorder
\preceq
in (
4.1.7
).

Lemma 7.2
.

For any
A=(a_{i,j}),M=(m_{i,j})\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N})_{r}
,
1\leq h\leq n-1
,
and
j,k\in[1,n]
,

(1)

if
a_{h+1,j}a_{h+1,k}\neq 0
(resp.
a_{h,j}a_{h,k}\neq 0
) and
j<k
, then
A^{+}_{h,j}\prec A^{+}_{h,k}
(resp.,

A^{-}_{h,k}\prec A^{-}_{h,j}
).

(2)

if
M\prec A
and
m_{h+1,k}>0
(resp.,
m_{h,k}>0
), then
M^{+}_{h,k}\prec A^{+}_{h,k}
(resp.,
M^{-}_{h,k}\prec A^{-}_{h,k}
).

We extend the order
\preceq
on the matrix set
M_{n}(\mathbb{N})
defined in (
4.1.7
) to
M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|\mathbb{N}_{2})
.
For
A^{\star}=(A^{\overline{0}}|A^{\overline{1}})\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})
, let

(7.2.1)

\mathscr{D}\!spt(A^{\overline{1}})=\{i\mid{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,i}=1,1\leq i\leq n\}

denote the
diagonal support
of
A^{\overline{1}}
. We follow the definition given in
[
GLL
, §6, (23)]
.

Definition 7.3
.

For any
A^{\star},B^{\star}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})
with base matrix
A,B
, respectively, define
A^{\star}\prec^{\star}B^{\star}
if
{A\prec B}
or

{A\approx B}
but
\mathscr{D}\!spt(A^{\star})\subset\mathscr{D}\!spt(B^{\star}).

We first determine the leading terms relative to
\preceq
for the products of the form
[{B^{\!\star}}]\cdot[{A^{\!\star}}]
, where
{B^{\!\star}}=E^{\star}_{h,\lambda,p}
or
E^{\star}_{\overline{h},\lambda}
or something mixed, where
{A^{\!\star}}
has an upper triangular base matrix
A=(a_{i,j})
satisfying further
a_{i,j}=0
for
i<j
and
j>k
for some fixed
1\leq k\leq n
, i.e,
A
has the
k^{\vartriangle}
-
shape

(7.3.1)

A=\begin{pmatrix}T_{k}&0\\
0&D_{n-k}\end{pmatrix}\text{ where $T_{k}$ is }k\times k\text{ upper triangular matrix, and }D_{n-k}\text{ diagonal.}

Parallelly, the leading term for
[{C^{\!\star}}]\cdot[{A^{\!\star}}]
, with
{C^{\!\star}}=F^{\star}_{h,\lambda,p}
or
F^{\star}_{\overline{h},\lambda}
or something mixed, will be discussed in Proposition
7.8
with
{A^{\!\star}}
being of the form (
7.7.1
).

In the following results, for
{M^{\!\star}}=(M^{\bar{0}}|M^{\bar{1}})
, the term
f_{M^{\!\star}}[{M^{\!\star}}]
in the expression of the form

f_{M^{\!\star}}[{M^{\!\star}}]+(\text{lower terms})_{\prec}\;\;(\text{resp}.,(\text{lower terms})_{\prec^{\star}})

is called the leading term, while“lower terms” relative to
\prec
(resp.,
\prec^{\star}
) stands for a linear combination of
[{B^{\!\star}}]
with
{B^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}
whose base
B\prec M^{\bar{0}}+M^{\bar{1}}
(resp., with
{B^{\!\star}}\prec^{\star}{M^{\!\star}}
).

Proposition 7.4
.

For
{A^{\!\star}}=({{a}^{\bar{0}}_{i,j}}|{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}})\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}
with base matrix
A=(a_{i,j})
, assume that
A
has the
k^{\vartriangle}
-shape in (
7.3.1
) for some

1\leq k\leq n
and

\mathrm{ro}(A)=\lambda
.
Then the following holds in
\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{\upsilon}(n,r)
for all
h<k
.

(1) If

{a}^{\bar{1}}_{h+1,k}=0
and
{a}^{\bar{0}}_{h+1,k}>0
, then, for all
p\in[1,{a}^{\bar{0}}_{h+1,k}]
,

\displaystyle{[E^{\star}_{h,\lambda,p}]\cdot[{A^{\!\star}}]}=\left[{{a}^{\bar{0}}_{h,k}+p}\atop p\right][({A}^{\bar{0}})^{+,p}_{h,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}]+(\text{lower terms})_{\prec}.

(2) If
{a}^{\bar{1}}_{h,k}={a}^{\bar{1}}_{h+1,k}=0
and
{a}^{\bar{0}}_{h+1,k}>0
, then

{[E^{\star}_{\overline{h},\lambda}]\cdot[{A^{\!\star}}]}=(-1)^{\wp({A^{\!\star}})+{\tilde{a}}^{\bar{1}}_{h-1,k}}[{A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{h+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}+E_{h,k}]+(\text{lower terms})_{\prec}.

(3) If
{a}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}=0
,
{a}^{\bar{0}}_{k,k}>0
and assume, in addition,
a_{i,k}=0
, for some
1\leq l<k\leq n
and all
l\leq i<k
. Then,
for any
p=p^{\bar{0}}+p^{\bar{1}}\in[1,{a}^{\bar{0}}_{k,k}]
with
p^{\overline{0}}\in\mathbb{N}
,
p^{\bar{1}}\in\{0,1\}
, we have

\displaystyle\Big([E^{\star}_{\bar{l},\lambda+{p}^{\bar{0}}\alpha_{l,k}+{p}^{\bar{1}}\alpha_{l+1,k}}]^{{p}^{\bar{1}}}

\displaystyle\cdot[E^{\star}_{l,\lambda+p\alpha_{l+1,k},{p}^{\bar{0}}}]\prod_{l<h<k}[E^{\star}_{h,\lambda+p\alpha_{h+1,k},p}]\Big)\cdot[{A^{\!\star}}]

\displaystyle=[{A}^{\bar{0}}-pE_{k,k}+{p}^{\bar{0}}E_{l,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}+{p}^{\bar{1}}E_{l,k}]+(\text{lower terms})_{\prec},

where
\alpha_{i,j}=\epsilon_{i}-\epsilon_{j}
, for
i\neq j
, and
\alpha_{i,i}=0
.

Proof.

(1) We first prove (1) by induction on
p
.
If
p=1
, by Lemma
4.1
(2), the shape of
A
together with
{a}^{\bar{1}}_{h+1,k}=0
(
h+1\leq k
) implies that the terms
[{B^{\!\star}}]
with base
B=A^{+}_{h,k}
appearing in the RHS of Lemma
4.1
(2) is unique. Thus, there exist
\textsc{c}_{B^{\!\star}}^{\lambda,1}\in{\mathcal{Z}}
such that

(7.4.1)

\displaystyle{[E^{\star}_{h,\lambda,1}][A^{\star}]}

\displaystyle=[{a}^{\bar{0}}_{h,k}+1][({A}^{\bar{0}})^{+,1}_{h,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{B^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r},B\approx A^{+}_{h,j},j<k\end{subarray}}\textsc{c}_{B^{\!\star}}^{\lambda,1}[{B^{\!\star}}]

\displaystyle=[{a}^{\bar{0}}_{h,k}+1][({A}^{\bar{0}})^{+,1}_{h,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{B^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r},B\prec A^{+}_{h,k}\end{subarray}}\textsc{c}_{B^{\!\star}}^{\lambda,1}[{B^{\!\star}}],

where the second equality follows from Lemma
7.2
(1). Thus, (1) holds for
p=1
.

Now assume
p>1
and (1) holds for
p-1
. Then, by induction,

(7.4.2)

\displaystyle{[E^{\star}_{h,\lambda,p-1}][{A^{\!\star}}]}=\left[{{a}^{\bar{0}}_{h,k}+p-1}\atop p-1\right][({A}^{\bar{0}})^{+,p-1}_{h,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{M^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
M\prec A^{+,p-1}_{h,k}\end{subarray}}\textsc{c}_{{M^{\!\star}}}^{\lambda,p-1}[{M^{\!\star}}].

The leading matrix
P^{\star}:=(({A}^{\bar{0}})^{+,p-1}_{h,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}})
also satisfies
{a}^{\bar{1}}_{h+1,k}=0
and its base matrix
P=A^{+,p-1}_{h,k}
has the same shape (
7.3.1
) as
A
. Thus, for
{\lambda}^{(p-1)}:=\mathrm{ro}(A^{+,p-1}_{h,k})=\lambda+(p-1)\alpha_{h}
, by applying (
7.4.1
) to
P^{\star}
,
we have

\displaystyle{[E^{\star}_{h,{\lambda}^{(p-1)},1}][P^{\star}]}=[E^{\star}_{h,{\lambda}^{(p-1)},1}][({A}^{\bar{0}})^{+,p-1}_{h,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}]={[{a}^{\bar{0}}_{h,k}+p]}[({A}^{\bar{0}})^{+,p}_{h,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{B^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
B\prec A^{+,p}_{h,k}\end{subarray}}\textsc{c}^{\lambda^{(p-1)},1}_{{B^{\!\star}}}[{B^{\!\star}}].

Multiplying the both sides of (
7.4.2
) by
\frac{1}{[p]}[E^{\star}_{h,{\lambda}^{(p-1)},1}]
and noting

\left[{{a}^{\bar{0}}_{h,k}+p-1}\atop p-1\right]\frac{[{a}^{\bar{0}}_{h,k}+p]}{[p]}=\left[{{a}^{\bar{0}}_{h,k}+p}\atop p\right]
, we obtain by (
7.1.1
)

(7.4.3)

\displaystyle{[E^{\star}_{h,\lambda,p}][{A^{\!\star}}]}=\left[{{a}^{\bar{0}}_{h,k}+p}\atop p\right][{A}^{\bar{0}}

\displaystyle-pE_{h+1,k}+pE_{h,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{B^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
B\prec A^{+,p}_{h,k}\end{subarray}}{{\textsc{c}^{\prime}}^{{\lambda}^{(p-1)},1}_{{B^{\!\star}}}}[{B^{\!\star}}]

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{M^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
M\prec A^{+,p-1}_{h,k}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{[p]}\textsc{c}^{\lambda,p-1}_{{M^{\!\star}}}[E^{\star}_{h,{\lambda}^{(p-1)},1}][{M^{\!\star}}]

where
{\textsc{c}^{\prime}}^{\lambda^{(p-1)},1}_{{B^{\!\star}}}=\frac{1}{[p]}\left[{{a}^{\bar{0}}_{h,k}+p-1}\atop p-1\right]\textsc{c}^{\lambda^{(p-1)},1}_{{B^{\!\star}}}\in\mathbb{Q}({\upsilon})
. Now, (1) follows from another application of Lemma
4.1
(2) which tells that, for every
M\prec A^{+,p-1}_{h,k}
,

[E^{\star}_{h,{\lambda}^{(p-1)},1}][{M^{\!\star}}]
is a linear combination of some
[{B^{\!\star}}]
with
B\prec M^{+}_{h,k}\prec(A^{+,p-1}_{h,k})^{+}_{h,k}=A^{+,p}_{h,k}
(Lemma
7.2
(2)).

For later use in this proof, we remark that the last order relation can be generalised to higher orders by a similar induction via Lemma
7.1
.

Corollary 7.5

(
Part 1).

Maintain the assumptions on
{A^{\!\star}}
in Proposition
7.4
(1) and assume
{M^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}
with
M\prec A
.
Then in
\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{\upsilon}(n,r)
we have

(7.5.1)

{[E^{\star}_{h,\mathrm{ro}(M),p}][M^{\star}]}=\text{lin. comb. of }[{B^{\!\star}}],B\prec A^{+,p}_{h,k}.

(2) We now prove part (2) of the proposition. If
{A^{\!\star}}
satisfies
{a}^{\bar{1}}_{h,k}={a}^{\bar{1}}_{h+1,k}=0
and
{a}^{\bar{0}}_{h+1,k}>0
(
h+1\leq k
) and has shape
(
7.3.1
) then, by Lemma
4.3
(1),

\displaystyle{[E^{\star}_{\bar{h},\lambda}][{A^{\!\star}}]}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{p({A^{\!\star}})+{\tilde{a}}^{\bar{1}}_{h-1,k}}[{A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{h+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}+E_{h,k}]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{B^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
B\approx A^{+}_{h,j},j<k\end{subarray}}\overline{\textsc{c}}_{{B^{\!\star}}}[{B^{\!\star}}]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{B^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
B\prec A^{+}_{h,j},j\leq k\end{subarray}}\overline{\textsc{c}}_{B^{\!\star}}[{B^{\!\star}}]

\displaystyle=(-1)^{p({A^{\!\star}})+{\tilde{a}}^{\bar{1}}_{h-1,k}}[{A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{h+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}+E_{h,k}]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{B^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
B\prec A^{+}_{h,k}\end{subarray}}\overline{\textsc{c}}_{{B^{\!\star}}}[{B^{\!\star}}],\;\;(\overline{\textsc{c}}_{B^{\!\star}}\in{\mathcal{Z}})

since
B\prec A^{+}_{h,j}\prec A^{+}_{h,k}
for
j<k
by Lemma
7.2
. This proves (2).

Here is the second part of Corollary
7.5
.

Corollary
7.5
(Part 2).

Maintain the assumption on
{A^{\!\star}}
in Proposition
7.4
(2). Then, for

{M^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}
with
M\prec A
, we have in
\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{\upsilon}(n,r)

(7.5.2)

{[E^{\star}_{\bar{h},\mathrm{ro}(M)}][M^{\star}]}=\text{lin. comb. of }[{B^{\!\star}}],B\prec A^{+}_{h,k}.

(3) Finally, we prove (3).
For
1\leq l<k
, we shall firstly show by downward induction on
l
that the following holds, for some
{\mathfrak{f}}_{{C^{\!\star}}}\in\mathbb{Q}({\upsilon})
,

(7.5.3)

\prod_{l<h<k}[E^{\star}_{h,\lambda+p\alpha_{h+1,k},p}][{A^{\!\star}}]=[{A}^{\bar{0}}+pE_{l+1,k}-pE_{k,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{C^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
C\prec A-pE_{k,k}+pE_{l+1,k}\end{subarray}}{\mathfrak{{f}}}_{{C^{\!\star}}}[{C^{\!\star}}].

Clearly (
7.5.3
) is trivially true in the case
l=k-1
(so,
l+1=k
), taking all
{\mathfrak{f}}_{{C^{\!\star}}}=0
. In the case
l=k-2
, since
{a}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}=0={a}_{k-1,k}
(additional assumption), and
a_{k,k}=a^{\bar{0}}_{k,k}\geq p
.
Applying Proposition
7.4
(1) yields

(7.5.4)

{[E^{\star}_{k-1,\lambda,p}][{A^{\!\star}}]}=[{A}^{\bar{0}}+pE_{k-1,k}-pE_{k,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{B^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
B\prec A+pE_{k-1,k}-pE_{k,k}\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{f}^{k-1,p}_{{B^{\!\star}}}[{B^{\!\star}}],

proving the case for
l=k-2
.

Assume now
l<k-2
and (3) is true for
l+1
:

(7.5.5)

\displaystyle{\prod_{l+1<h<k}[E^{\star}_{h,\lambda+p\alpha_{h+1,k},p}][{A^{\!\star}}]}=[{A}^{\bar{0}}+pE_{l+2,k}-pE_{k,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{M^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
M\prec A-pE_{k,k}+pE_{l+2,k}\end{subarray}}{\mathfrak{f}^{\prime}}_{{M^{\!\star}}}[{M^{\!\star}}].

Multiplying the both sides of (
7.5.5
) by
[E^{\star}_{l+1,\lambda+p\alpha_{l+2,k},p}]
gives the LHS of (
7.5.3
) and the right hand side
[E^{\star}_{l+1,\gamma,p}][{A}^{\bar{0}}+pE_{l+2,k}-pE_{k,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}]+
(a linear comb. of
[E^{\star}_{l+1,\gamma,p}][{M^{\!\star}}]
), where
\gamma:=\mathrm{ro}(A-pE_{k,k}+pE_{l+2,k})=\lambda+p\alpha_{l+2,k}
.

Observe that the matrix
({A}^{\bar{0}}+pE_{l+2,k}-pE_{k,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}})
in (
7.5.5
) satisfies
{a}^{\bar{0}}_{l+1,k}={a}^{\bar{1}}_{l+1,k}={a}^{\bar{1}}_{l+2,k}=0,{a}^{\bar{0}}_{l+2,k}>0
and moreover the base matrix

A+pE_{l+2,k}-pE_{k,k}
is of the form as (
7.3.1
). So, applying Proposition
7.4
(1) gives rise to

\displaystyle{[E^{\star}_{l+1,\gamma,p}][{A}^{\bar{0}}+pE_{l+2,k}-pE_{k,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}]}=[{A}^{\bar{0}}+pE_{l+1,k}-pE_{k,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P^{\star}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
P\prec A+pE_{l+1,k}-pE_{k,k}\end{subarray}}{\mathfrak{f}}^{l+1,p}_{P^{\star}}[P^{\star}],

for some
{\mathfrak{f}}^{l+1,p}_{P^{\star}}\in\mathbb{Q}({\upsilon})
. On the other hand, for
{M^{\!\star}}
with
M\prec{}^{\prime}\!A:=A+pE_{l+2,k}-pE_{k,k}
in (
7.5.5
), we have
\mathrm{ro}(M)=\gamma
and, by
(
7.5.1
) and noting
{}^{\prime}\!A^{+,p}_{l+1,k}=A+pE_{l+1,k}-pE_{k,k}
,

{[E^{\star}_{l+1,\gamma,p}]{[{M^{\!\star}}]}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N^{\star}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
N\prec A+pE_{l+1,k}-pE_{k,k}\end{subarray}}{\mathfrak{f}^{\prime}}^{l+1,p}_{N^{\star}}[N^{\star}].

Hence, (
7.5.3
) follows.

To complete the proof of (3), we now multiply the RHS of (
7.5.3
) by the remaining factor
[E^{\star}_{\bar{l},\lambda+{p}^{\bar{0}}\alpha_{l,k}+{p}^{\bar{1}}\alpha_{l+1,k}}]^{{p}^{\bar{1}}}[E^{\star}_{l,\lambda+p\alpha_{l+1,k},{p}^{\bar{0}}}]
. We shall first consider the multiplication with the leading term in RHS of (
7.5.3
).
Observe that the leading matrix
({A}^{\bar{0}}-pE_{k,k}+pE_{l+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}})
in (
7.5.3
) is of the form (
7.3.1
) with
a^{\bar{1}}_{l,k}=a^{\bar{1}}_{l+1,k}=0
and

a^{\bar{0}}_{l+1,k}=p\geq p^{\bar{0}},a^{\bar{0}}_{l,k}=0
. Moreover, its base matrix is
A-pE_{k,k}+pE_{l+1,k}
with
\mathrm{ro}(A-pE_{k,k}+pE_{l+1,k})=\lambda+p\alpha_{l+1,k}
.
Then applying (1) yields

(7.5.6)

\displaystyle{[E^{\star}_{l,\lambda+p\alpha_{l+1,k},{{p}^{\bar{0}}}}]}

\displaystyle[{A}^{\bar{0}}-pE_{k,k}+pE_{l+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}]=[{A}^{\bar{0}}-pE_{k,k}+{p}^{\bar{1}}E_{l+1,k}+{p}^{\bar{0}}E_{l,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}]

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{C^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
C\prec A-pE_{k,k}+{p}^{\bar{1}}E_{l+1,k}+{p}^{\bar{0}}E_{l,k}\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{f}^{{l,{{p}^{\bar{0}}}}}_{{C^{\!\star}}}[{C^{\!\star}}].

If
{p}^{\bar{1}}=0
, then
p={p}^{\bar{0}}
and the multiplication of
[E^{\star}_{\bar{l},\lambda+{p}^{\bar{0}}\alpha_{l,k}+{p}^{\bar{1}}\alpha_{l+1,k}}]^{{p}^{\bar{1}}}[E^{\star}_{l,\lambda+p\alpha_{l+1,k},{p}^{\bar{0}}}]
with the leading term in RHS of (
7.5.3
) is completed. For each
[C^{\star}]
appearing in the lower terms in RHS of (
7.5.3
), using (
7.5.1
) in Corollary
7.5
gives

(7.5.7)

[E^{\star}_{l,\lambda+p\alpha_{l+1,k},{p}^{\bar{0}}}][{C^{\!\star}}]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}D^{\star}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
D\prec A-pE_{k,k}+{p}^{\bar{1}}E_{l+1,k}+{p}^{\bar{0}}E_{l,k}\end{subarray}}{\mathfrak{f}^{\prime}}^{{l,{{p}^{\bar{0}}}}}_{D^{\star}}[D^{\star}].

This together with (
7.5.6
) proves (3) in this situation.

If
{p}^{\bar{1}}=1
, then we need further to compute the multiplication of the
RHS
of (
7.5.6
) by
[E^{\star}_{\bar{l},\lambda+{p}^{\bar{0}}\alpha_{l,k}+{p}^{\bar{1}}\alpha_{l+1,k}}]
. Thus, we apply (2) for the leading term and (
7.5.2
) for the lower terms.

By the additional assumption on matrix
A
, the parity value
\wp({{A^{\!\star}}})={\tilde{a}}^{\bar{1}}_{l,k}
. Hence, for
P^{\star}:=({A}^{\bar{0}}-pE_{k,k}+{p}^{\bar{1}}E_{l+1,k}+{p}^{\bar{0}}E_{l,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}})
with base matrix
P
, we have

\wp({P^{\star}})=\wp({{A^{\!\star}}})={\tilde{a}}^{\bar{1}}_{l,k}
and
\mathrm{ro}(P)=\lambda+{p}^{\bar{0}}\alpha_{l,k}+{p}^{\bar{1}}\alpha_{l+1,k}
. Then, by (2), we have

(7.5.8)

\displaystyle{[E^{\star}_{\bar{l},\mathrm{ro}(P)}][P^{\star}]}=[{A}^{\bar{0}}-{p}E_{k,k}+{p}^{\bar{0}}E_{l,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}+{p}^{\bar{1}}E_{l,k}]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{B^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
B\prec A-pE_{k,k}+pE_{l,k}\end{subarray}}{\mathfrak{f}^{\prime}}^{\overline{l}}_{{B^{\!\star}}}[{B^{\!\star}}].

For any
{C^{\!\star}}
in (
7.5.6
),
C\prec P
and

P^{+}_{l,k}=A-pE_{k,k}+{p}^{\bar{1}}E_{l+1,k}+{p}^{\bar{0}}E_{l,k}+E_{l,k}-E_{l+1,k}=A-pE_{k,k}+pE_{l,k}
.
Thus, by (
7.5.2
),

(7.5.9)

\displaystyle{[E^{\star}_{\overline{l},\mathrm{ro}(P)}][{C^{\!\star}}]}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{B^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
B\prec A-pE_{k,k}+pE_{l,k}\end{subarray}}{\mathfrak{\tilde{f}}}^{\overline{l}}_{{B^{\!\star}}}[{B^{\!\star}}].

For each
[C^{\star}]
appearing in the lower terms in RHS of (
7.5.3
) in this case, using Corollary
7.5
(1)–(2) one can obtain a formula similar to (
7.5.7
). Then
combining (
7.5.6
), (
7.5.8
) and (
7.5.9
) proves (3) in this situation.
∎

We now determine the leading term of the products
[D^{\star}_{\bar{h},\mathrm{ro}(A)}][A^{\bar{0}}|A^{\bar{1}}]
, where both
A^{\bar{0}}
and
A^{\bar{1}}
are upper triangular.

Proposition 7.6
.

Suppose
{A^{\!\star}}=({{a}^{\bar{0}}_{i,j}}|{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}})\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}
has an upper triangular base
A
(i.e.,
{{a}^{\bar{0}}_{i,j}}+{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}}=0\;\forall i>j
) and assume
{a}^{\bar{0}}_{h,h}>0
and
{a}^{\bar{1}}_{h,h}=0

for some
h\in[1,n]
. Then the following holds in
\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{\upsilon}(n,r)
:

(7.6.1)

\displaystyle{{[{D^{\star}_{\bar{h},\lambda}}]}[{A^{\!\star}}]}={(-1)}^{\wp({A^{\!\star}})+{\widetilde{a}}^{\overline{1}}_{h,h}}{{\upsilon}}^{d_{h}(A,h)}

\displaystyle[{{A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{h,h}|{A}^{\bar{1}}+E_{h,h}}]+(\text{lower terms})_{\prec^{\star}}.

In particular, for
h=n
, we have

(7.6.2)

{{[{D^{\star}_{\bar{n},\lambda}}]}[{A^{\!\star}}]}=[{{A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{n,n}|{A}^{\bar{1}}+E_{n,n}}].

Proof.

By the hypothesis,
A^{\star}_{h}:=({{A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{h,h}|{A}^{\bar{1}}+E_{h,h}})\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}
and its base is
A
. On the other hand,
({{A}^{\bar{0}}+E_{h,h}|{A}^{\bar{1}}-E_{h,h}})\notin M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}
which means
[{{A}^{\bar{0}}+E_{h,h}|{A}^{\bar{1}}-E_{h,h}}]=0
by the convention right after (
4.0.1
).
Thus, the hypothesis and Lemma
4.2
(2) imply

\displaystyle{[{D^{\star}_{\bar{h},\lambda}}][{A^{\!\star}}]}

\displaystyle={(-1)}^{\wp({A^{\!\star}})+{\widetilde{a}}^{\overline{1}}_{h,h}}{{\upsilon}}^{d_{h}(A,h)}[{{A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{h,h}|{A}^{\bar{1}}+E_{h,h}}]

\displaystyle+\sum_{k>h}{(-1)}^{\wp({A^{\!\star}})+{\widetilde{a}}^{\overline{1}}_{h,k}}{{\upsilon}}^{d_{h}(A,k)}\Big\{[{{A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{h,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}+E_{h,k}}]+[a_{h,k}]_{{\upsilon}^{2}}[{{A}^{\bar{0}}+E_{h,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}-E_{h,k}}]\Big\}

\displaystyle+\sum_{{B^{\!\star}},{B}\prec{{A}}}{{\textsc{p}}_{{{D^{\star}_{\bar{h},\lambda}}},{A^{\!\star}},{B^{\!\star}}}}[{{B^{\!\star}}}].

If
h=n
, then
A
satisfies the SDP condition. Thus, both summations do not occur (one may apply Lemma
4.2
(1)
directly), proving (
7.6.2
).

It remains to prove that
A^{\star}_{h}
is a leading term when
h<n
.
For those
{B^{\!\star}}
with
B\prec A
, by Definition
7.3
, we have

{B^{\!\star}}\prec^{\star}A^{\star}_{h}
. If (at most) one of the matrices
({{A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{h,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}+E_{h,k}})
and
({{A}^{\bar{0}}+E_{h,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}-E_{h,k}})
, for
k>h
, is in
M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}
, call it
A^{\star}_{k}
. We have, by (
7.2.1
),

\mathscr{D}\!spt(A^{\star}_{k})=\mathscr{D}\!spt({A^{\!\star}})\subset\mathscr{D}\!spt({A^{\!\star}})\cup\{h\}=\mathscr{D}\!spt(A^{\star}_{h}).

Hence, by Definition
7.3
,
A^{\star}_{k}\prec^{\star}A^{\star}_{h}
. This proves (
7.6.1
).
∎

With a similar argument, we also have the following.

Corollary 7.7
.

Maintain the assumption on
{A^{\!\star}}
and
h
as in Proposition
7.6
.
For any
{M^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}

with
M^{\star}\prec^{\star}{A^{\!\star}}
, we have in
\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{\upsilon}(n,r)

[D^{\star}_{\bar{h},\mathrm{ro}(M)}][{M^{\!\star}}]=\text{lin. comb. of }[{B^{\!\star}}],{B^{\!\star}}\prec^{\star}({A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{h,h}|{A}^{\bar{1}}+E_{h,h}).

Finally, we determine the leading term of the products
[{C^{\!\star}}]\cdot[{A^{\!\star}}]
, where
{C^{\!\star}}=F^{\star}_{h,\lambda,p}
or
F^{\star}_{\overline{h},\lambda}
or something mixed and the base matrix
A
satisfies
a_{i,j}=0
for
i>j
and
j<k
for certain
1\leq k\leq n
, i.e
A
has the
k_{\triangledown}
-shape:

(7.7.1)

A=\begin{pmatrix}T_{k-1}&B\\
0&C\end{pmatrix}\text{ where $T_{k-1}$ is upper triangular and $B,C$ arbitrary}.

Recall the
-
-matrix (of higher order)
A^{-,p}_{h,k}=A-pE_{h,k}+pE_{h+1,k}
defined in (
7.0.2
). Note that
A^{-,p}_{h,k}
is the base matrix of
(({A}^{\bar{0}})^{-,p}_{h,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}})
.

Proposition 7.8
.

Assume
{A^{\!\star}}=({{a}^{\bar{0}}_{i,j}}|{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}})\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}
and its base matrix
A=(a_{i,j})
satisfies
a_{i,j}=0
for
i>j
and
j<k
(i.e.,
A
has the shape in (
7.7.1
)), for some
k\in[1,n]
. Then, putting
\mathrm{ro}(A)=\lambda
, the following holds in
\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{\upsilon}(n,r)

for any given
h\in[1,n]
satisfying
h\geq k
.

(1) If
{a}^{\bar{1}}_{h,k}=0
in the case
h>k
(no requirement in the case
h=k
) and
a_{h,k}^{\bar{0}}>0
then

\displaystyle{[F^{\star}_{h,\lambda,p}][{A^{\!\star}}]}={\upsilon}^{-p{a}^{\bar{1}}_{h,k}}\left[{{a}^{\bar{0}}_{h+1,k}+p}\atop p\right][({A}^{\bar{0}})^{-,p}_{h,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}]+(\text{lower terms})_{\prec^{\star}}.

(2) If
{a}^{\bar{1}}_{h,k}=0
in case
h>k
,
{a}^{\bar{1}}_{h+1,k}=0

and
{a}^{\bar{0}}_{h,k}>0
, then

{[F^{\star}_{\bar{h},\lambda}][{A^{\!\star}}]}=(-1)^{p({A^{\!\star}})+{{\tilde{a}}^{\bar{1}}_{h,k}}}{\upsilon}^{-{a}^{\bar{1}}_{h,k}}[{A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{h,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}+E_{h+1,k}]+(\text{lower terms})_{\prec^{\star}}.

(3) If
{a}^{\bar{0}}_{k,k}>0
and, in addition, assume
a_{i,k}=0
for all
i
with
k<i\leq l+1
for some
l
with
k\leq l<n
.
Then, for any
p={p}^{\bar{0}}+{p}^{\bar{1}}\in[1,{a}^{\bar{0}}_{k,k}]
with
{p}^{\bar{0}}\in\mathbb{N}
and
{p}^{\bar{1}}\in\{0,1\}
, we have

\displaystyle{[F^{\star}_{\bar{l},\lambda+{p}^{\bar{0}}\alpha_{l+1,k}+\alpha_{l,k}}]}^{{p}^{\bar{1}}}

\displaystyle[F^{\star}_{l,\lambda+p\alpha_{l,k},{p}^{\bar{0}}}]\prod_{l>h\geq k}[F^{\star}_{h,\lambda+p\alpha_{h,k},p}][{A^{\!\star}}]=(-1)^{p^{\bar{1}}(\wp({{A^{\!\star}}})+{\tilde{a}}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k})}{\upsilon}^{-{p}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}}

\displaystyle\cdot[{A}^{\bar{0}}-pE_{k,k}+{p}^{\bar{0}}E_{l+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}+{p}^{\bar{1}}E_{l+1,k}]+(\text{lower terms})_{\prec^{\star}}.

Proof.

(1) We first prove (1)
by induction on
p
via (
7.1.2
). We need to consider two cases: (i)
h>k
and
{a}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}=0
and (ii)
h=k
and
{a}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}\not=0
.
The proof of Case (i) is entirely similar to the proof of Proposition
7.4
(1) with the
E
-notations replaced by the
F
-notations, noting that the hypothesis implies
{a}^{\bar{1}}_{h,k}=0
, for all
h\geq k
.

We now assume
h=k
and
{a}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}=1
. We again apply induction on
p
. If
p=1
, applying Lemma
4.1
(3)

(7.8.1)

\displaystyle{[F^{\star}_{k,\lambda,1}][{A^{\!\star}}]}

\displaystyle={\upsilon}^{-{a}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}}[{a}^{\bar{0}}_{k+1,k}+1][{A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{k,k}+E_{k+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}]+{\upsilon}^{{a}^{\bar{0}}_{k,k}}[{A}^{\bar{0}}|{A}^{\bar{1}}-E_{k,k}+E_{k+1,k}]

\displaystyle-({\upsilon}-{\upsilon}^{-1}){\upsilon}^{{a}^{\bar{0}}_{k,k}}\left[a_{k+1,k}+1\atop 2\right][{A}^{\bar{0}}+2E_{k+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}-E_{k,k}-E_{k+1,k}]\}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{B^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
B\approx A^{-}_{k,j},j>k\end{subarray}}{\mathfrak{f}^{{k,1}}_{{B^{\!\star}}}}[{B^{\!\star}}]\;\;\big(\mathfrak{f}^{{k,1}}_{{B^{\!\star}}}\in\mathbb{Q}({\upsilon})\big)

has three terms with the same leading base matrix
A^{-}_{k,k}
. Since

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}\!spt({A}^{\bar{0}}|{A}^{\bar{1}}-E_{k,k}+E_{k+1,k})=\mathscr{D}\!spt({A}^{\bar{0}}+2E_{k+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}-E_{k,k}-E_{k+1,k})=\mathscr{D}\!spt({A^{\!\star}})\setminus\{k\}

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}\!spt({A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{k,k}+E_{k+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}})=\mathscr{D}\!spt({A^{\!\star}}),

it follows from Definition
7.3
that

\displaystyle({A}^{\bar{0}}|{A}^{\bar{1}}-E_{k,k}+E_{k+1,k})\prec^{\star}({A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{k,k}+E_{k+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}),

\displaystyle({A}^{\bar{0}}+2E_{k+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}-E_{k,k}-E_{k+1,k})\prec^{\star}({A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{k,k}+E_{k+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}).

Hence (
7.8.1
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle{[F^{\star}_{k,\lambda,1}][{A^{\!\star}}]}

\displaystyle={\upsilon}^{-{a}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}}[{a}^{\bar{0}}_{k+1,k}+1][{A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{k,k}+E_{k+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{B^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
{B^{\!\star}}\prec^{\star}({A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{k,k}+E_{k+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}})\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{f}^{{k,1}}_{{B^{\!\star}}}[{B^{\!\star}}],

proving the inductive base.

Assume now
p>1
and (1) is correct for
p-1
(under the conditions
h=k
and
{a}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}=1
):

(7.8.2)

\displaystyle{[F^{\star}_{k,\lambda,p-1}][{A^{\!\star}}]}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\upsilon}^{-(p-1){a}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}}\left[{{a}^{\bar{0}}_{k+1,k}+p-1}\atop{p-1}\right][{A}^{\bar{0}}-(p-1)E_{k,k}+(p-1)E_{k+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}]

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{C^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
{C^{\!\star}}\prec^{\star}({A}^{\bar{0}}-(p-1)E_{k,k}+(p-1)E_{k+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}})\end{subarray}}{\mathfrak{f}^{{k,p-1}}_{{C^{\!\star}}}}[{C^{\!\star}}].

Observe that the base matrix of
P^{\star}:=({A}^{\bar{0}}-(p-1)E_{k,k}+(p-1)E_{k+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}})
appearing in (
7.8.2
) has the form as (
7.7.1
) and its row vector is
\mu:=\lambda-(p-1)\alpha_{k}
. Then we can apply the proof for the
p=1
case to obtain

(7.8.3)

\displaystyle[F^{\star}_{k,\mu,1}][P^{\star}]={\upsilon}^{-{a}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}}[{a}^{\bar{0}}_{k+1,k}+p][{A}^{\bar{0}}-pE_{k,k}+pE_{k+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{B^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
{B^{\!\star}}\prec^{\star}{({A}^{\bar{0}}-pE_{k,k}+pE_{k+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}})}\end{subarray}}{{{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}}^{{k,1}}_{B^{\star}}}[{B^{\!\star}}].

For any lower term
[{C^{\!\star}}]
appearing in (
7.8.2
), since
{C^{\!\star}}\prec({A}^{\bar{0}}-(p-1)E_{k,k}+(p-1)E_{k+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}})
,
it follows by Lemma
4.1
(3) that

(7.8.4)

\displaystyle{[F^{\star}_{k,\mu,1}][{C^{\!\star}}]}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{B^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
{B^{\!\star}}\prec^{\star}({A}^{\bar{0}}-pE_{k,k}+pE_{k+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}})\end{subarray}}{{{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime\prime}}^{{k,1}}_{B^{\star}}}[{B^{\!\star}}].

Combining (
7.8.2
), (
7.8.3
) and (
7.8.4
) proves (1) (in case (ii)).

(2) The proof of (2) is a bit more complicated, comparing with Proposition
7.4
(2).
According to the assumption, the matrix
{A^{\!\star}}
satisfies
a_{i,j}=0
for
i>j
and
j<k
and moreover,
{a}^{\bar{1}}_{h,k}=0
, for
h>k
,
{a}^{\bar{1}}_{h+1,k}=0
, and
{a}^{\bar{0}}_{h,k}>0
. Then, by Lemma
4.3
(2), there exist some
\mathfrak{f}^{{\bar{h}}}_{A^{\prime\star}}\in\mathbb{Q}({\upsilon})
,

(7.8.5)

\displaystyle[F^{\star}_{\bar{h},\lambda}][{A^{\!\star}}]=(-1)^{\wp({A^{\!\star}})+{\tilde{a}}^{\bar{1}}_{h,k}}{\upsilon}^{-{a}^{\bar{1}}_{h,k}}[{A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{h,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}+E_{h+1,k}]

\displaystyle+{\delta_{h,k}}(-1)^{\wp({A^{\!\star}})+{\tilde{a}}^{\bar{1}}_{h,k}}{\upsilon}^{{a}^{\bar{0}}_{h,k}}[{{a}^{\bar{0}}_{h+1,k}+1}][{{A}^{\bar{0}}+E_{h+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}-E_{h,k}}]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A^{\prime\star}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
A^{\prime}\approx A^{-}_{h,j},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}j>k}\end{subarray}}{\mathfrak{f}^{{\bar{h}}}_{A^{\prime\star}}}[A^{\prime\star}]

\displaystyle+({\upsilon}-{\upsilon}^{-1})\text{\rm HH}\overline{\textsc{f}}(h,{A^{\!\star}})+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{B^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
B\prec A^{-}_{h,j},j>k\end{subarray}}{{\textsc{p}}_{F^{\star}_{\bar{h},\lambda},{A^{\!\star}},{B^{\!\star}}}}[{B^{\!\star}}],

where
\text{\rm HH}\overline{\textsc{f}}(h,{A^{\!\star}})
is a linear combination of the basis vectors
[C^{\star}_{k^{\prime},l}]=[{C}^{\bar{0}}_{k^{\prime}}\pm E_{h+1,l}|{C}^{\bar{1}}_{k^{\prime}}\mp E_{h+1,l}]

which have base matrix
C_{k^{\prime},l}=A^{-}_{h,k^{\prime}}
with
1\leq l<k^{\prime}\leq n
.

Since
a_{h,k^{\prime}}=a_{h+1,k^{\prime}}=0
for
k^{\prime}<k{(\leq h)}
, these matrices
A^{-}_{h,k^{\prime}}
and
C^{\star}_{k^{\prime},l}
are only defined (i.e., belong to
M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|\mathbb{N}_{2})
) for
k\leq k^{\prime}
and
k\leq l
, respectively. Hence,
\text{\rm HH}\overline{\textsc{f}}(h,{A^{\!\star}})
is a linear combination of the basis vectors
[C^{\star}_{k^{\prime},l}]
, for
k\leq l<k^{\prime}
, with
base matrix
C_{k^{\prime},l}=A^{-}_{h,k^{\prime}}\prec A^{-}_{h,k}
as
k^{\prime}>k
.
In other words,

\text{\rm HH}\overline{\textsc{f}}(h,{A^{\!\star}})
is a linear combination of
[{C^{\!\star}}]
with
C\prec A^{-}_{h,k}
and hence, by definition,
C^{\star}\prec^{\star}(A^{\bar{0}}-E_{h,k}|A^{\bar{1}}+E_{h+1,k})
, the leading matrix.

Meanwhile, for these
M^{\star}
appearing in the two summations in (
7.8.5
), since
M\approx A^{-}_{h,j}
or
M\prec A^{-}_{h,j}
with
j>k
, we clearly have
M^{\star}\prec^{\star}({A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{h,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}+E_{h+1,k})
as
A^{-}_{h,j}\prec A^{-}_{h,k}
.

Finally, it remains to deal with the term in (
7.8.5
) involving
\delta_{h,k}
when
h=k
and
{a}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}=1
. Though
({A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{h,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}+E_{h+1,k})
and
({{A}^{\bar{0}}+E_{h+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}-E_{h,k}})
have the same base matrix
A^{-}_{h,k}
,
but we have

\mathscr{D}\!spt({{A}^{\bar{0}}+E_{k+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}-E_{k,k}})=\mathscr{D}\!spt({A^{\!\star}})\setminus\{k\}\subset\mathscr{D}\!spt({A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{k,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}+E_{k+1,k}).

Hence,

({{A}^{\bar{0}}+E_{h+1,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}-E_{h,k}})\prec^{\star}({A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{h,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}+E_{h+1,k}).

Hence, this proves that (
7.8.5
) has the required form as in (2).

Like Corollary
7.5
, similar arguments for proving (1) and (3) gives the following result.

Corollary 7.9
.

Keep the hypotheses of Proposition
7.8
on the matrix
A
and assume
{M^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|\mathbb{N}_{2})_{r}
satisfying
{M^{\!\star}}\prec^{\star}{A^{\!\star}}
. Then we have (with the hypothesis in Proposition
7.8
(1))

(7.9.1)

\displaystyle{[F^{\star}_{h,\mathrm{ro}(M),p}][M^{\star}]}=\text{lin. comb. of }[{B^{\!\star}}],{B^{\!\star}}\prec^{\star}(({A}^{\bar{0}})^{-,p}_{h,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}),\;\;\forall p\in[1,a_{h,k}^{\bar{0}}],

and (with the hypothesis in Proposition
7.8
(2))

(7.9.2)

\displaystyle{[F^{\star}_{\bar{h},\mathrm{ro}(M)}][M^{\star}]}=\text{lin. comb. of }[{B^{\!\star}}],{B^{\!\star}}\prec^{\star}({A}^{\bar{0}}-E_{h,k}|{A}^{\bar{1}}+E_{h+1,k}).

(3) Finally, with the preparation of (1) and (2) and Corollary
7.9
, the proof of (3) is entirely similar to the proof of Proposition
7.4
(3) using the extended order
\preceq^{\star}
.
∎

We are now ready to introduce the aforementioned monomials and associated triangular relations in
\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{\upsilon}(n,r)
.
We need a linear order over the following set to define certain monomials (cf. the order on
[
DDPW
, p.562]
):

\mathscr{I}_{n}:=\{(i,j)\mid 1\leq i,j\leq n\}=\mathscr{I}_{n}^{<}\sqcup\mathscr{I}_{n}^{=}\sqcup\mathscr{I}_{n}^{>}.

where
\mathscr{I}_{n}^{<}=\{(i,j)\in\mathscr{I}_{n}\mid i<j\}
,
\mathscr{I}_{n}^{=}=\{(i,j)\in\mathscr{I}_{n}\mid i=j\}
and
\mathscr{I}_{n}^{>}=\{(i,j)\in\mathscr{I}_{n}\mid i>j\}
.

Definition 7.10
.

We linearly order
\mathscr{I}_{n}^{<}
by setting,
for
(i,j),(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\mathscr{I}^{<}
,

(i,j)<(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\iff\text{either }j>j^{\prime}\text{ or, if }j=j^{\prime},\text{ then }i>i^{\prime}.

Then, linearly order
\mathscr{I}_{n}^{=}=\{(i,i)\mid i\in[1,n]\}
by setting

(i,i)<(i^{\prime},i^{\prime})\iff i<i^{\prime},

and linearly order
\mathscr{I}_{n}^{>}
by setting,
for
(i,j),(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\mathscr{I}^{>}
,

(i,j)<(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\iff\text{either }j<j^{\prime}\text{ or, if }j=j^{\prime},\text{ but }i<i^{\prime}.

Finally, for
(i,j)\in\mathscr{I}^{<}
,
(i^{\prime},i^{\prime})\in\mathscr{I}_{n}^{=}
and
(i^{\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime})\in\mathscr{I}^{>}
, we set
(i,j)>(i^{\prime},i^{\prime})>(i^{\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime})
.

For example, the ordering on

\mathscr{I}_{4}
is given by
(2,1)<(3,1)<(4,1)<(3,2)<(4,2)<(4,3)<(1,1)<(2,2)<(3,3)<(4,4)<(3,4)<(2,4)<(1,4)<(2,3)<(1,3)<(1,2)
.

For any given
{A^{\!\star}}=({a}^{\bar{0}}_{i,j}|{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j})\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}
with base matrix
A=(a_{i,j})
, we now introduce the elements
\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{i,j}

(see Definition
7.11
below), for all
i,j\in\mathscr{I}_{n}
, and use the linear order on
\mathscr{I}_{n}
to define the following products:

(7.10.1)

\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}={\prod_{{1}\leq j\leq{n-1}}}\Big({\prod_{j+1\leq i\leq n}}\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{i,j}\Big)\cdot{\prod_{1\leq j\leq n}\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{j,j}\cdot{\prod_{{n}\geq j\geq{1}}}\big({\prod_{{j-1}\geq i\geq{1}}}\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{i,j}\big)},

where product order follows the convention in (
7.0.1
). In other words, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}

\displaystyle:=(\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{2,1}\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{3,1}\cdots\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{n,1})(\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{3,2}\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{4,2}\cdots\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{n,2})\cdots(\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{n,n-1})

\displaystyle\cdot(\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{1,1}\cdots\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{n-1,n-1}\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{n,n})\cdot(\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{n-1,n}\cdots\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{2,n}\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{1,n})\cdots(\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{2,3}\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{1,3})(\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{1,2}).

Definition 7.11

(for
\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{i,j}
)
.

We set
\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{i,j}=1
, for all
(i,j)\in\mathscr{I}_{n}
with
a_{i,j}=0
, and define
\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{i,j}
downward recursively on the well-ordered subset

\mathscr{I}_{n}({A^{\!\star}}):=\{(i,j)\in\mathscr{I}_{n}\mid a_{i,j}\neq 0\}\cup{\{(1,2)\}}:

(1)

For the largest element
{(1,2)}
in
\mathscr{I}_{n}({A^{\!\star}})
, set

\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{1,2}=\begin{cases}[E^{\star}_{\overline{1},\mathrm{co}(A)-{a}^{\bar{0}}_{1,2}\alpha_{1}}]^{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{1,2}}[E^{\star}_{1,\mathrm{co}(A),{a}^{\bar{0}}_{1,2}}],&\text{if }{a}_{1,2}>0;\\
[D^{\star}_{\mathrm{co}(A)}],&\text{if }{a}_{1,2}=0.\end{cases}

(2)

Suppose, for
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\mathscr{I}_{n}({A^{\!\star}})
and
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\leq{(1,2)}
,
\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}
is defined with
\gamma_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}=\mathrm{ro}(\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}})
,
and
(i,j)
is an immediate predecessor of
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
in
\mathscr{I}_{n}({A^{\!\star}})
. Then, set

\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{i,j}\!=\!\begin{cases}[E^{\star}_{\bar{i},\gamma_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}+{a}^{\bar{0}}_{i,j}\alpha_{i,j}+{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}\alpha_{i+1,j}}]^{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}}[E^{\star}_{i,\gamma_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}+a_{i,j}\alpha_{i+1,j},{a}^{\bar{0}}_{i,j}}]\prod_{i<h<j}[E^{\star}_{h,\gamma_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}+a_{i,j}\alpha_{h+1,j},a_{i,j}}],\!&\mbox{if }i<j;\\
[D^{\star}_{\bar{j},\gamma_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}}]^{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{j,j}},\!&\text{if }i=j;\\
{[F^{\star}_{\overline{i-1},\gamma_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}+{a}^{\bar{0}}_{i,j}\alpha_{i,j}+{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i-1,j}\alpha_{i,j}}]^{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}}[F^{\star}_{i-1,\gamma_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}+p\alpha_{i-1,j},{a}^{\bar{0}}_{i,j}}]\prod_{i>h\geq j}[F^{\star}_{h,\gamma_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}+a_{i,j}\alpha_{h-1,j},a_{i,j}}]},\!&\mbox{if }i>j,\end{cases}

where
\alpha_{i,j}=\epsilon_{i}-\epsilon_{i+1}
for all
i,j\in[1,n]
.

Note from the definition that the right most term of
\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{i,j}
for
i<j
is
[E^{\star}_{h,\gamma_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}},a_{i,j}}]
as
\alpha_{j,j}=0
and
\mathrm{co}(E^{\star}_{h,\gamma_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}},a_{i,j}})=\gamma_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}
.
Associated with
{A^{\!\star}}=({a}^{\bar{0}}_{i,j}|{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j})\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}
and
(l,k)\in\mathscr{I}_{n}
, we may describe the leading term of the partial product
\prod_{(l,k)\leq(i,j)}\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{i,j}
over the interval
[(l,k),(1,2)]:=\{(i,j)\in\mathscr{I}_{n}\mid(l,k)\leq(i,j)\}
.

Definition 7.12

(for matrices
{}^{\ }_{l}\!A^{\star}_{k}
)
.

We start with
[D^{\star}_{\mu}]=[\mu|O]
with
\mathrm{co}(A)=\mu
and set

{}^{\ }_{1}\!A^{\star}_{2}
to be the matrix defining the leading term of
\mathbf{m}_{1,2}^{({A^{\!\star}})}[D^{\star}_{\mu}]
. For
(l,k)<(1,2)
, define

{}^{\ }_{l}\!A^{\star}_{k}=({b}^{\bar{0}}_{i,j}|{b}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j})
by moving certain entries from
{A^{\!\star}}
to the corresponding positions in
{}^{\ }_{l}\!A^{\star}_{k}
as follows:

If
l<k
(i.e.,
(l,k)\in\mathscr{I}_{n}^{<}
), then the entries of
{}^{\ }_{l}\!A^{\star}_{k}
are defined by setting

(1)

{b}^{\bar{0}}_{i,j}={a}^{\bar{0}}_{i,j}
and
{b}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}={a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}
for
i<j<k
and for
j=k
but
i\leq l
;

(2)

{b}^{\bar{0}}_{j,j}=\mu_{j}-\sum_{i<j}a_{i,j}={\sum_{i\geq j}a_{i,j}}
for
j<k
,
{b}^{\bar{0}}_{k,k}=\sum_{i>l}a_{i,k}
, and
{b}^{\bar{0}}_{j,j}=\mu_{j}
for
j>k
;

(3)

the remaining entries are 0.

If
l=k
(i.e.,
(l,k)\in\mathscr{I}_{n}^{=}
), set

(7.12.1)

{}^{\ }_{k}\!A^{\star}_{k}=({}^{\ }_{n-1}\!A^{\bar{0}}_{n}-\sum_{j\geq k}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{j,j}E_{j,j}|{}^{\ }_{n-1}\!A^{\bar{1}}_{n}+\sum_{j\geq k}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{j,j}E_{j,j}).

If
l>k
(i.e.,
(l,k)\in\mathscr{I}_{n}^{>}
), the entries of
{}^{\ }_{l}\!A^{\star}_{k}=({b}^{\bar{0}}_{i,j}|{b}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j})
are defined by setting

(a)

b^{\bar{0}}_{i,j}=0=b_{i,j}^{\bar{1}}
, for
i>j
and
j<k
or for
j=k
but
k<i<l
;

(b)

{b}^{\bar{0}}_{j,j}=\sum_{i>j}a_{i,j}+{a}^{\bar{0}}_{j,j}
for
j<k
;
{b}^{\bar{0}}_{k,k}={a}^{\bar{0}}_{k,k}+\sum_{k<i<l}a_{i,k}\;\;(a_{i,j}={a}^{\bar{0}}_{i,j}+{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j})
;

(c)

all remaining entries are the same as the corresponding entries of
{A^{\!\star}}
.

Observe from the definition the following:

The least
(l,k)
satisfying
l<k
is
(n-1,n)
. Thus, if we use the canonical decomposition

(7.12.2)

M=M_{-}+M_{0}+M_{+}

of a square matrix
M
into a sum of lower triangular, diagonal, and upper triangular parts, then the entries in the upper triangular part

A^{\star}_{+}=({A}^{\bar{0}}_{+}|A^{\bar{1}}_{+})
are all in the corresponding positions of
{}^{\ }_{n-1}\!A^{\star}_{n}
.

The least
(l,k)
satisfying
l=k
is
(1,1)
. Note that
{}^{\ }_{1}\!A^{\star}_{1}
is the matrix such that the entires in
A^{\bar{0}}_{+}
and
A^{\bar{1}}_{0}+A^{\bar{1}}_{+}
are all in the corresponding positions of
{}^{\ }_{1}\!A^{\star}_{1}
.

Finally, the least
(l,k)
satisfying
l>k
is
(2,1)
. By the definition, we see that
{}^{\ }_{2}\!A^{\star}_{1}={A^{\!\star}}
.

Theorem 7.13
.

For any
{A^{\!\star}}=({a}^{\bar{0}}_{i,j}|{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j})\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}
with base matrix
A=(a_{i,j})
,
we have

{\bf m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}=(-1)^{\sum_{l>k}a^{\bar{1}}_{l,k}(\wp({A^{\!\star}})-{\widetilde{a}}^{\bar{1}}_{l,k})+\sum_{i\leq j,j>k}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}}{\upsilon}^{\sum_{l>k}a^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}(a_{k,l}-a_{l,k})}[{A^{\!\star}}]+(\text{lower terms})_{\prec^{\star}}.

In particular, the set
\{{\bf m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}\mid{A^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\}
forms a basis for
\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{\upsilon}(n,r)
.

Proof.

For a fixed
(l,k)\in\mathscr{I}_{n}
, let

(7.13.1)

\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}|_{(l,k)}:=\prod_{(l,k)\leq(i,j)\leq(1,2)}\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{i,j}.

be the product
over the interval
[(l,k),(1,2)]
and, for
(i,j)\in\mathscr{I}_{n}
, let

\mathfrak{f}^{i,j}_{{A^{\!\star}}}=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }i<j\text{ or }i=j=n;\\
{(-1)^{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,i}{\sum_{s\leq t,t>i}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{s,t}}}{\upsilon}^{\sum_{t>i}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,i}a_{i,t}},}&\text{if }{i=j<n;}\\
(-1)^{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}(\wp({{A^{\!\star}}})-{\tilde{a}}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j})}{\upsilon}^{-{a}_{i,j}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{j,j}},&\text{if }i>j.\end{cases}

We claim that

(7.13.2)

\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}|_{(l,k)}=\mathsf{lc}^{(l,k)}[{}^{\ }_{l}\!A^{\star}_{k}]+(\text{lower terms})_{\prec^{\star}}.

Here
\mathsf{lc}^{(l,k)}=\Big(\prod_{(i,j)\geq(l,k)}\mathfrak{f}^{i,j}_{{A^{\!\star}}}\Big)
is the leading coefficient at
(l,k)
and
“
(\text{lower terms})_{\prec^{\star}}
” means a linear combination of
[{M^{\!\star}}]
with
{M^{\!\star}}\prec^{\star}{}^{\ }_{l}\!A^{\star}_{k}
, for
{M^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}
.

To prove the claim, we apply induction on the well-ordered set
(\mathscr{I}_{n},\leq)
(or, more precisely, on
(\mathscr{I}_{n}({A^{\!\star}}),\leq)
).

For the inductive base
(l,k)=(1,2)
, if
a_{1,2}=0
, then, by Definition
7.11
(1),

\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{1,2}=[D^{\star}_{\mathrm{co}(A)}]=[{}^{\ }_{1}\!A^{\star}_{2}].

If
a_{1,2}>0
, applying Proposition
7.4
(3) yields

\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{1,2}=[E^{\star}_{\overline{1},\mathrm{co}(A)-{a}^{\bar{0}}_{1,2}\alpha_{1}}]^{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{1,2}}[E^{\star}_{1,\mathrm{co}(A),{a}^{\bar{0}}_{1,2}}]=[{}^{\ }_{1}\!A^{\star}_{2}]+(\text{lower terms})_{\prec^{\star}}.

Assume now
(l^{\prime},k^{\prime})\leq(1,2)
and the claim is true at
(l^{\prime},k^{\prime})
:

(7.13.3)

\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}|_{(l^{\prime},k^{\prime})}=\mathsf{lc}^{(l^{\prime},k^{\prime})}[{}^{\ }_{l^{\prime}}\!A^{\star}_{k^{\prime}}]+(\text{lower terms})_{\prec^{\star}}.

Let
(l,k)
be the immediate predecessor of
(l^{\prime},k^{\prime})
in
\mathscr{I}_{n}({A^{\!\star}})
.
We now prove that the claim is true at
(l,k)
. In other words,
\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}|_{(l,k)}=\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{l,k}\cdot\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}|_{(l^{\prime},k^{\prime})}
has the leading term

[{}^{\ }_{l}\!A^{\star}_{k}]
. There are three cases to consider.

Case 1 (
l<k
)
.
In this case, for any
(l,k)<(i,j)<(1,2)
, we have
i<j
and so
\mathfrak{f}^{i,j}_{{A^{\!\star}}}=1
. By the definition linear ordering on
\mathscr{I}_{n}({A^{\!\star}})
,
(l^{\prime},k^{\prime})
must be one of the following two situations:

(1a)

if
l=1
, then
k>2
and
(l^{\prime},k^{\prime})=(k-2,k-1)
;

(1b)

if
l>1
, then
(l^{\prime},k^{\prime})=(l-1,k)
.

In both cases, by Definition
7.12
(1)–(3), the matrices involved have the shape as in (
7.3.1
). Thus,
applying Proposition
7.4
yields,

\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{l,k}[{}^{\ }_{l^{\prime}}\!A^{\star}_{k^{\prime}}]=[{}^{\ }_{l}\!A^{\star}_{k}]+(\text{lower terms})_{\prec}.

For a lower term
[{C^{\!\star}}]
with
C\prec{}^{\ }_{l^{\prime}}\!A_{k^{\prime}}
, the base of
{}^{\ }_{l^{\prime}}\!A^{\star}_{k^{\prime}}
, by Definition
7.3
, repeatedly applying Corollary
7.5
gives

\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{l,k}[{C^{\!\star}}]=\text{lin. comb. of }[{M^{\!\star}}],M\prec{}^{\ }_{l}\!A_{k},

proving (
7.13.2
) in this case.

Case 2 (
l=k
).
By the definition of linear ordering on
\mathscr{I}_{n}({A^{\!\star}})
,
(l^{\prime},k^{\prime})
must be one of the following two situations:

(2a)

if
l=n
, then
k=n
and
(l^{\prime},k^{\prime})=(n-1,n)
;

(2b)

if
l<n
, then
(l^{\prime},k^{\prime})=(k+1,k+1)
.

The proof of (2a) uses (
7.6.2
) with leading coefficient 1. This case can be done similarly by Proposition
7.6
and Corollary
7.7
.

If
k<n
and
(l^{\prime},k^{\prime})=(k+1,k+1)
, by (
7.12.1
), the base matrix
B
of
{B^{\!\star}}={}^{\ }_{k+1}\!A^{\star}_{k+1}
is with
b_{i,j}=0
for
i>j
and
{b}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}=0
, moreover,
({B}^{\bar{0}}-{a}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}E_{k,k}|{B}^{\bar{1}}+{a}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}E_{k,k})={}^{\ }_{k}\!A^{\star}_{k}
.
By Proposition
7.6
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{k,k}[{}^{\ }_{k+1}\!A^{\star}_{k+1}]=(-1)^{a_{k,k}^{\bar{1}}(\wp({}^{\ }_{k+1}\!A^{\star}_{k+1})+{\tilde{b}}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k})}{\upsilon}^{a_{k,k}^{\bar{1}}\sum_{t>k}b_{k,t}}[{}^{\ }_{k}\!A^{\star}_{k}]+\text{(lower terms)}_{\prec^{\star}}.

Since
\wp({}^{\ }_{k+1}\!A^{\star}_{k+1})=\sum_{i<j}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}+\sum_{j>k}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{j,j}
,
{\tilde{b}}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}=\sum_{i<j\leq k}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}
and

\sum_{t>k}b_{k,t}=\sum_{t>k}a_{k,t}
,

\displaystyle(-1)^{a_{k,k}^{\bar{1}}(\wp({}^{\ }_{k+1}\!A^{\star}_{k+1})+{\tilde{b}}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k})}{\upsilon}^{a_{k,k}^{\bar{1}}\sum_{t>k}b_{k,t}}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{a_{k,k}^{\bar{1}}(\sum_{i<j}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}+\sum_{j>k}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{j,j}+\sum_{i<j\leq k}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j})}{\upsilon}^{a_{k,k}^{\bar{1}}\sum_{t>k}a_{k,t}}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{a_{k,k}^{\bar{1}}\sum_{i\leq j,j>k}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}}{\upsilon}^{a_{k,k}^{\bar{1}}\sum_{t>k}a_{k,t}}=\mathfrak{f}^{k,k}_{{A^{\!\star}}}.

For lower term
[{C^{\!\star}}]
with
{C^{\!\star}}\prec{}^{\ }_{k+1}\!A^{\star}_{k+1}
in (
7.13.3
), applying Corollary
7.7

proves (
7.13.2
) in this case.

Case 3
l>k
.
By the definition linear ordering on
\mathscr{I}_{n}({A^{\!\star}})
,
(l^{\prime},k^{\prime})
must be one of the following two situations:

(3a)

if
l=n
, then
k=n-1
and
(l^{\prime},k^{\prime})=(1,1)
or
k<n-1
and
(l^{\prime},k^{\prime})=(k+2,k+1)
;

(3b)

if
l<n
, then
(l^{\prime},k^{\prime})=(l+1,k)
.

If
(l^{\prime},k^{\prime})=(1,1)
, the base matrix
B
of
{B^{\!\star}}={}^{\ }_{1}\!A^{\star}_{1}
has the shape as in (
7.7.1
), and

({B}^{\bar{0}}-a_{n,n-1}E_{n-1,n-1}+{a}^{\bar{0}}_{n,n-1}E_{n,n-1}|{B}^{\bar{1}}+{a}^{\bar{1}}_{n,n-1}E_{n,n-1})={}^{\ }_{n}\!A^{\star}_{n-1}.

Applying Proposition
7.8
(3), due to
\wp({{}^{\ }_{1}\!A^{\star}_{1}})+{\tilde{b}}^{\bar{1}}_{n-1,n-1}=2{\tilde{b}}^{\bar{1}}_{n-1,n-1}+\wp({{A^{\!\star}}})-{\tilde{a}}^{\bar{1}}_{n,n-1}
,
{b}^{\bar{1}}_{n-1,n-1}={a}^{\bar{1}}_{n-1,n-1}
and definition of
\mathfrak{f}^{n,n-1}_{{A^{\!\star}}}
above, we have

(7.13.4)

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{n,n-1}[{}^{\ }_{1}\!A^{\star}_{1}]

\displaystyle=(-1)^{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{n,n-1}(\wp({{A^{\!\star}}})-{\tilde{a}}^{\bar{1}}_{n,n-1})}{\upsilon}^{-{a}_{n,n-1}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{n-1,n-1}}[{}^{\ }_{n}\!A^{\star}_{n-1}]+(\text{lower terms})_{\prec^{\star}}.

Thus, the first case in (3a) follows from Corollary
7.9
.

If
(l^{\prime},k^{\prime})=(k+2,k+1)
, by Definition
7.12
(a)–(c), the base matrix
B
of
{B^{\!\star}}={}^{\ }_{k+2}\!A^{\star}_{k+1}
has the shape as in (
7.7.1
), and
({B}^{\bar{0}}-a_{n,k}E_{k,k}+{a}^{\bar{0}}_{n,k}E_{n,k}|{B}^{\bar{1}}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{n,k}}E_{n,k})={}^{\ }_{n}\!A^{\star}_{k}
. Thus, this subcase can be proved similarly by Proposition
7.8
(3)
and Corollary
7.9
.

Finally, if
(l^{\prime},k^{\prime})=(l+1,k)
, the base matrix
B
of
{B^{\!\star}}={}^{\ }_{l+1}\!A^{\star}_{k}
still has the shape as in (
7.7.1
), and
({B}^{\bar{0}}-a_{l,k}E_{k,k}+{a}^{\bar{0}}_{l,k}E_{n,k}|{B}^{\bar{1}}+{a}^{\bar{1}}_{l,k}E_{l,k})={}^{\ }_{l}\!A^{\star}_{k}
. Applying Proposition
7.8
(3),

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{l,k}[{}^{\ }_{l+1}\!A^{\star}_{k}]

\displaystyle=(-1)^{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{l,k}(\wp({{}^{\ }_{l+1}\!A^{\star}_{k}})+{\tilde{b}_{k,k}}^{\bar{1}})}{\upsilon}^{-a_{l,k}{b}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}}[{}^{\ }_{l}\!A^{\star}_{k}]+(\text{lower terms})_{\prec^{\star}}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{f}^{l,k}_{{A^{\!\star}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{}^{\ }_{l}\!A^{\star}_{k}]}+(\text{lower terms})_{\prec^{\star}},

noting that
\wp({{}^{\ }_{l+1}\!A^{\star}_{k}})+{\tilde{b}_{k,k}}^{\bar{1}}=2{\tilde{b}}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}+\wp({{A^{\!\star}}})-{\tilde{a}}^{\bar{1}}_{l,k}
,
{b}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}={a}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}
and
\mathfrak{f}^{l,k}_{{A^{\!\star}}}=(-1)^{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{l,k}(\wp({{A^{\!\star}}})-{\tilde{a}}^{\bar{1}}_{l,k})}{\upsilon}^{-a_{l,k}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}}.

Since
\mathsf{lc}^{(l,k)}=\mathfrak{f}^{l,k}_{{A^{\!\star}}}\mathsf{lc}^{(l^{\prime},k^{\prime})}
, applying Corollary
7.9
proves the claim (
7.13.2
) in this
l>k
case. This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Corollary 7.14
.

The
\mathbb{Q}({\upsilon})
-algebra

\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{\upsilon}(n,r)
is generated by

(7.14.1)

\{[E^{\star}_{h,\lambda}],[E^{\star}_{\bar{h},\lambda}],[F^{\star}_{h,\lambda}],[F^{\star}_{\bar{h},\lambda}],[D^{\star}_{\lambda}],[D^{\star}_{\bar{i},\lambda}]\mid\lambda\in\Lambda(n,r),1\leq h<n,i\in[1,n]\}.

Proof.

By Lemma
7.1
,
[p]^{!}[E^{\star}_{h,p,\lambda}]
(resp.,
[p]^{!}[F^{\star}_{h,p,\lambda}]
) is a product of some
[E^{\star}_{h,\mu}]

(resp.,
[F^{\star}_{h,\mu}]
). Thus, Theorem
7.13
implies that, for any
{A^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}
,
[{A^{\!\star}}]
is in the subalgebra generated by

\{[E^{\star}_{h,\lambda}],[E^{\star}_{\bar{h},\lambda}],[F^{\star}_{h,\lambda}],[F^{\star}_{\bar{h},\lambda}],[D^{\star}_{\bar{i},\lambda}],[D^{\star}_{\lambda}]\mid\lambda\in\Lambda(n,r),1\leq h<n,i\in[1,n]\}.

Now the assertion follows from Corollary
3.6
.
∎

8.
Mapping
{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})
onto a superalgebra of formal infinite series

Recall from Proposition
5.1
that
{O}(\bm{0},r)
is the identity element in
\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{\upsilon}(n,r)
.
Define the superalgebra

{\bm{\mathcal{Q}}_{\upsilon}^{s}(n)}:=\prod_{r\geq 1}{\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{\upsilon}(n,r)}.

The elements
(x_{r})_{r\geq 1}
in
\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{{\upsilon}}({n})

are written as
formal infinite series

\sum_{r\geq 1}x_{r}

which is homogenous of degree
\bar{i}
if every
x_{r}
is homogenous of degree
\bar{i}
.
In particular, the identity element

(8.0.1)

1=\sum_{{\lambda}\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}}[D^{\star}_{\lambda}]=\sum_{r\geq 1}1_{r},\qquad[D^{\star}_{\lambda}][D^{\star}_{\mu}]=\delta_{{\lambda},\mu}[D^{\star}_{\lambda}],\;\forall{\lambda},\mu\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\setminus\{\bm{0}\},

where
1_{r}=\sum_{{\lambda}\in\Lambda(n,r)}[D^{\star}_{\lambda}]
is the identity element of
\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{\upsilon}(n,r)
.
For any
{A^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}^{\pm}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2}),\bm{j}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{n}
(see (
5.0.1
)),
let

(8.0.2)

{{A^{\!\star}}}(\bm{j}):=\sum_{r\geq 1}{A}(\bm{j},r)=\sum_{r\geq|A|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\Lambda(n,r-|{A}|)\end{subarray}}{v}^{\lambda*\bm{j}}[{A}^{\bar{0}}+\lambda|{A}^{\bar{1}}]\in{\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{{\upsilon}}({n})},

a formal infinite series,
and form the superspace

(8.0.3)

{\bm{\mathfrak{A}}_{v}(n)}=\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{Q}({v})}\{{{A^{\!\star}}}(\bm{j})\ \ \bm{|}\ {A^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}^{\pm}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2}),\bm{j}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{n}\}\subset{\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{{\upsilon}}({n})}.

Note that
1=(O|O)({\bf 0})\in{\bm{\mathfrak{A}}_{v}(n)}
.

The linear independence of the elements
{A^{\!\star}}(\bm{j})
can be proved by a method
similar to the proof of
[
DF2
, Proposition 4.1(2)]
or
[
DLZ
, Th. 8.2]
.

Lemma 8.1
.

The set
\mathfrak{L}=\{{{A^{\!\star}}}(\bm{j})\ \bm{|}\ {A^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}^{\pm}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2}),\bm{j}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{n}\}
is a
\mathbb{Q}({v})
-basis of
{\bm{\mathfrak{A}}_{v}(n)}
.

We introduce the following subset of generators in
{\bm{\mathfrak{A}}_{v}(n)}
:

(8.1.1)

\mathscr{G}_{n}=\{G_{i},G_{i}^{-1},G_{\overline{i}},X_{j},X_{\overline{j}},Y_{j},Y_{\overline{j}}\ \bm{|}\ 1\leq i\leq n,\ 1\leq j\leq n-1\},

where

(8.1.2)

\displaystyle G_{i}^{\pm 1}=({O}|{O})(\pm\epsilon_{i}),\qquad X_{j}=({E_{j,j+1}}|{O})(\bm{0}),\qquad Y_{j}=({E_{j+1,j}}|{O})(\bm{0}),

\displaystyle G_{\overline{i}}=({O}|{E_{i,i}})(\bm{0}),\qquad X_{\overline{j}}=({O}|{E_{j,j+1}})(\bm{0}),\qquad Y_{\overline{j}}=({O}|{E_{j+1,j}})(\bm{0}).

For notational clarity, we also write
\overline{G}_{i}=G_{\overline{i}},\overline{X}_{i}=X_{\overline{j}}
and
\overline{Y}_{i}=Y_{\overline{j}}
.

The following result is immediate from Corollary
6.4
together with the fact that the coefficients
{\textsc{p}}_{{B^{\!\star}},\bm{j}}(Z_{\bullet},{A^{\!\star}})
are independent of
r\geq|A|
.

Lemma 8.2
.

For any
{A^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}^{\pm}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})
and
Z\in\mathscr{G}_{n}
, there exist some
{\textsc{p}}_{{B^{\!\star}},\bm{j}}(Z,{A^{\!\star}})\in\mathbb{Q}({v})
such that in
\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{{\upsilon}}({n})

(8.2.1)

\displaystyle Z\cdot{{A^{\!\star}}}(\bm{0})

\displaystyle=\sum_{{B^{\!\star}},\bm{j}^{\prime}}{\textsc{p}}_{{B^{\!\star}},\bm{j}^{\prime}}(Z,{A^{\!\star}}){{B^{\!\star}}}(\bm{j}^{\prime}),

where
{B^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}^{\pm}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})
,
\bm{j}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{n}
. In particular, for any
\bm{j}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, there exists
a_{\bm{j}}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that

(8.2.2)

{{A^{\!\star}}}(\bm{j})={\upsilon}^{a_{\bm{j}}}\cdot(\prod_{h=1}^{n}G_{h}^{{j}_{h}})\cdot{{A^{\!\star}}}(\bm{0}).

Proof.

The last assertion follows from the fact

(8.2.3)

\displaystyle G_{i}^{\pm}\cdot{{A^{\!\star}}}(\bm{j})=v^{\pm\mathrm{ro}(A)*\epsilon_{i}}{{A^{\!\star}}}(\bm{j}\pm\epsilon_{i}),\quad{{A^{\!\star}}}(\bm{j})\cdot G_{i}^{\pm}=v^{\pm\mathrm{co}(A)*\epsilon_{i}}{{A^{\!\star}}}(\bm{j}\pm\epsilon_{i}),

see Proposition
5.1
.
∎

We are now ready to show the Main Theorem
1.4
in the introduction by the following two steps: (1) Prove the image of
\bm{\xi}_{n}
is
{\bm{\mathfrak{A}}_{v}(n)}
, and (2) Prove
\bm{\xi}_{n}
is injective. We complete (1) in this section and leave (2) to the next section.

For any
{}^{\prime}\!\!{A^{\!\star}}=(a^{\bar{0}}_{i,j}|a^{\bar{1}}_{i,j})\in M_{n}^{\pm}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})
, define

(8.2.4)

\textsc{m}_{i,j}^{{}^{\prime}\!\!{A^{\!\star}}}=\begin{cases}{\overline{X}_{i}}^{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}}X_{i}^{({a}^{\bar{0}}_{i,j})}X_{i+1}^{({a}_{i,j})}\cdots X_{j-2}^{({a}_{i,j})}X_{{j-1}}^{({a}_{i,j})},&\mbox{for all }1\leq i<j\leq n;\\
\overline{G}_{i}^{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,i}},&\mbox{for all}1\leq i=j\leq n;\\
{\overline{Y}_{i-1}}^{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}}Y_{i-1}^{({a}^{\bar{0}}_{i,j})}Y_{i-2}^{({a}_{i,j})}\cdots Y_{j+1}^{({a}_{i,j})}Y_{{j}}^{({a}_{i,j})},&\mbox{for all }1\leq j<i\leq n.\end{cases}

Here, for any
Z\in\mathscr{G}_{n}
and positive integer
a
,
Z^{(a)}=\frac{Z^{a}}{[a]!}
denotes the divided power
and we set
Z^{(a)}=1
in the case
a=0
.

We also define some monomials in the generators in
\mathscr{G}_{n}
, using the product order convention in (
7.0.1
).
For
{}^{\prime}\!\!{A^{\!\star}}
as above and
\bm{j}=(j_{1},\ldots,j_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, use the well-ordered set
(\mathscr{I}_{n},\leq)
(Definition
7.10
) to define

(8.2.5)

\displaystyle{{\textsc{m}}^{{}^{\prime}\!\!{A^{\!\star}},\bf 0}}

\displaystyle:=\prod_{j\in[1,n)}^{<}\Big({\prod_{j+1\leq i\leq n}}\textsc{m}^{{}^{\prime}\!\!{A^{\!\star}}}_{i,j}\Big)\cdot{\prod_{1\leq j\leq n}\textsc{m}^{{}^{\prime}\!\!{A^{\!\star}}}_{j,j}\cdot\prod_{j\in[1,n]}^{>}\big({\prod_{{j-1}\geq i\geq{1}}}\textsc{m}^{{}^{\prime}\!\!{A^{\!\star}}}_{i,j}\big)}=\prod_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(i,j)\in(\mathscr{I}_{n},\leq)}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textsc{m}}_{i,j}^{{}^{\prime}\!\!{A^{\!\star}}},

\displaystyle\textsc{m}^{{}^{\prime}\!\!{A^{\!\star}},\bm{j}}

\displaystyle:=G_{1}^{j_{1}}\cdots G_{n}^{j_{n}}{\textsc{m}}^{{}^{\prime}\!\!{A^{\!\star}},\bf 0}.

Here, for
j=1
, the product
\prod_{0\geq i\geq 1}
is understood as 1.

By Proposition
5.2
and Remark
5.6
(2),
we have, for
a\in\mathbb{N},i\in[1,n)
,

(8.2.6)

X_{i}^{(a)}=(aE_{i,i+1}|O)(\bm{0}),\;\;Y_{i}^{(a)}=(aE_{i+1,i}|O)(\bm{0}).

Thus, if
{A^{\!\star}}={}^{\prime}\!\!{A^{\!\star}}+({\rm{diag}}(a_{1,1}^{\bar{0}},\cdots,a_{n,n}^{\bar{0}})|O)\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}
has base
A
, then, by (
8.0.1
)

(8.2.7)

\textsc{m}^{{}^{\prime}\!\!{A^{\!\star}},\bf 0}\cdot[D^{\star}_{{\mathrm{co}(A)}}]={\bf m}^{({A^{\!\star}})},\;\;\text{the monomial defined in \eqref{m(A)}},

and

\textsc{m}_{r}^{{}^{\prime}\!\!{A^{\!\star}},\bf 0}:={\textsc{m}}^{{}^{\prime}\!\!{A^{\!\star}},\bf 0}\cdot 1_{r}
is the
r
-th component of
\textsc{m}^{{}^{\prime}\!\!{A^{\!\star}},\bf 0}
in
\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{\upsilon}(n,r)
.

Note that (
8.2.7
) can be seen as follows. First,

\textsc{m}^{{}^{\prime}\!\!{A^{\!\star}}}_{1,2}[D^{\star}_{\mathrm{co}(A)}]=\mathbf{m}_{1,2}^{({A^{\!\star}})}
(see Definition
7.11
). Assume
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\geq(1,2)
and, for

\textsc{m}^{{}^{\prime}\!\!{A^{\!\star}},\bf 0}|_{(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})}
as similarly defined in (
7.13.2
),
\textsc{m}^{{}^{\prime}\!\!{A^{\!\star}},\bf 0}|_{(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})}\cdot[D^{\star}_{{\mathrm{co}(A)}}]={\bf m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}|_{(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})}
. If
\mathrm{ro}({\bf m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}|_{(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})})=\gamma_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}
and
(i,j)
is
immediate predecessor of
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
, then

\textsc{m}^{{}^{\prime}\!\!{A^{\!\star}}}_{i,j}\cdot[D^{\star}_{\gamma_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}}]=\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}_{i,j}.
This proves (
8.2.7
) by induction.

Theorem 8.3
.

(1) For any
{A^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}^{\pm}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})
, we have in
\bm{\mathcal{Q}}_{{\upsilon}}{({n})}

(8.3.1)

\displaystyle{\textsc{m}}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bf 0}=\mathsf{lc}^{A^{\!\star}}{{A^{\!\star}}}(\bm{0})+\sum_{\!\!{B^{\!\star}}\prec{A^{\!\star}},\ \bm{j}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\mathfrak{f}^{{A^{\!\star}}}_{{B^{\!\star}},\bm{j}}{{B^{\!\star}}}(\bm{j}),

for some
\mathfrak{f}^{{A^{\!\star}}}_{{B^{\!\star}},\bm{j}}\in\mathbb{Q}({v})
, where

\mathsf{lc}^{A^{\!\star}}={(-1)^{\sum_{l>k}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{l,k}(\wp({{A^{\!\star}}})-{\tilde{a}}^{\bar{1}}_{l,k})+\sum_{i\leq j,j>k}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}}{\upsilon}^{\sum_{l>k}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}(a_{k,l}-a_{l,k})}}.

(2) The
\mathbb{Q}({v})
-space
{\bm{\mathfrak{A}}_{v}(n)}
is a subalgebra of
\bm{\mathcal{Q}}_{{\upsilon}}{({n})}
generated by the set
\mathscr{G}_{n}
defined in (
8.1.1
). Moreover, the set

\{\textsc{m}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bm{j}}\mid{A^{\!\star}}\in M^{\pm}_{n}(\mathbb{N}|\mathbb{N}_{2}),\bm{j}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}\}
forms a basis for
{\bm{\mathfrak{A}}_{v}(n)}
.

(3) There is a superalgebra epimorphism
\bm{\xi}_{n}:{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})\to{\bm{\mathfrak{A}}_{v}(n)}
defined by

{{\mathsf{E}}}_{j}\mapsto X_{j},\;{{\mathsf{E}}}_{\overline{j}}\mapsto X_{\overline{j}},\;{{\mathsf{F}}}_{j}\mapsto Y_{j},\;{{\mathsf{F}}}_{\overline{j}}\mapsto Y_{\overline{j}},\;{{\mathsf{K}}}_{i}^{\pm 1}\mapsto G_{i}^{\pm 1},\;{{\mathsf{K}}}_{\overline{i}}\mapsto G_{\overline{i}},

for all
1\leq i\leq n,1\leq j\leq n-1
.

Proof.

(1) With the notation introduced above, we have, by Theorem
7.13
,

\displaystyle\textsc{m}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bf 0}1_{r}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda(n,r-|A|)}{\textsc{m}}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bf 0}\cdot[D^{\star}_{\mathrm{co}(A+{\lambda})}]=\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda(n,r-|A|)}{\bf m}^{({A^{\!\star}}+{\lambda})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda(n,r-|A|)}\mathfrak{f}_{({A}^{\bar{0}}+\lambda|{A}^{\bar{1}})}[{A}^{\bar{0}}+\lambda|{A}^{\bar{1}}]+\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda(n,r-|A|)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}C^{\star}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
C^{\star}\prec^{\star}({A}^{\bar{0}}+\lambda|{A}^{\bar{1}})\end{subarray}}{{\mathfrak{f}}_{A^{\star}+{\lambda},C^{\star}}}[C^{\star}]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{lc}^{A^{\!\star}}{A^{\!\star}}({\bf 0},r)+\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda(n,r-|A|)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}C^{\star}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
C^{\star}\prec^{\star}{A^{\!\star}}\end{subarray}}{{\mathfrak{f}}_{A^{\star}+{\lambda},C^{\star}}}[C^{\star}],

where
\mathsf{lc}^{A^{\!\star}}=\mathfrak{f}_{({A}^{\bar{0}}+\lambda|{A}^{\bar{1}})}={(-1)^{\sum_{l>k}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{l,k}(\wp({{A^{\!\star}}})-{\tilde{a}}^{\bar{1}}_{l,k})+\sum_{i\leq j,j>k}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}}{\upsilon}^{\sum_{l>k}{a}^{\bar{1}}_{k,k}(a_{k,l}-a_{l,k})}}
is independent of
r
. Thus,

\textsc{m}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bf 0}=\mathsf{lc}^{A^{\!\star}}{A^{\!\star}}({\bf 0})+\sum_{r\geq|A|}\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda(n,r-|A|)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}C^{\star}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})_{r}\\
C^{\star}\prec^{\star}{A^{\!\star}}\end{subarray}}{{\mathfrak{f}}_{A^{\star}+{\lambda},C^{\star}}}[C^{\star}].

On the other hand, by Lemma
8.2
,
\textsc{m}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bf 0}
is a linear combination of
{B^{\!\star}}(\bm{j})
. Consequently, the triple summation above is a linear combination of
{B^{\!\star}}(\bm{j})
with
{B^{\!\star}}\prec^{\star}{A^{\!\star}}
, proving (
8.3.1
).

(2) By (
8.3.1
) and (
8.2.2
), there exists
a_{A^{\!\star}}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that

{\textsc{m}}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bm{j}}=\pm{\upsilon}^{a_{A^{\!\star}}}{{A^{\!\star}}}(\bm{j})+\sum_{\!\!{B^{\!\star}}\prec^{\star}{A^{\!\star}},\ \bm{j}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\mathfrak{f^{\prime}}^{{A^{\!\star}}}_{{B^{\!\star}},\bm{j}^{\prime}}{{B^{\!\star}}}(\bm{j}^{\prime}).

This relation shows that every
A(\bm{j})
is in the subalgebra generated by
\mathscr{G}_{n}
and all
{\textsc{m}}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bm{j}}
forms a basis.

(3) Note that the map
\bm{\xi}_{n}
is induced by the homomorphisms
\bm{\xi}_{n,r}
given in Theorem
6.3
. So, it is well-defined. The surjectivity follows from (2).
∎

Corollary 8.4
.

For each
r\geq 0
, the homomorphism
\bm{\xi}_{n,r}:{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})\to\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{\upsilon}(n,r)
in Theorem
6.3
is surjective. Moreover
\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{\upsilon}(n,r)
is generated by

\mathscr{G}_{n,r}
(see (
6.3.4
)) and spanned by
\{{A^{\!\star}}(\bm{j},r)\mid{A^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}^{\pm}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2}),\bm{j}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}\}
.

Proof.

We first observe that if
\pi_{r}
denotes the canonical projection from
\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{{\upsilon}}({n})
onto its
r
-th component, then
\bm{\xi}_{n,r}=\pi_{r}|_{{\bm{\mathfrak{A}}_{v}(n)}}\circ\bm{\xi}_{n}
.
Note also that, for
\lambda\in\Lambda(n,r)
, each element
G_{\lambda}:=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left[G_{i}\atop\lambda_{i}\right]
with
{\left[G_{i}\atop\lambda_{i}\right]}=\prod_{t=1}^{i}\frac{G_{i}{\upsilon}^{-t+1}-G_{i}{\upsilon}^{t-1}}{{\upsilon}^{t}-{\upsilon}^{-t}}
has an inverse image in
{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})
and

\pi_{r}(G_{\lambda})=[\lambda|O]=[D^{\star}_{\lambda}]
, by
[
DDPW
, (13.9.3)]
. Thus, the set

(8.4.1)

\{\textsc{m}^{{}^{\prime}\!\!{A^{\!\star}},\bf 0}G_{\mathrm{co}(A)}\mid{A^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|\mathbb{N}_{2})_{r}\}\subset\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{{\upsilon}}({n})

is a subset of the image of
\bm{\xi}_{n}
. By (
8.2.7
) and Theorem
7.13
and noting
{}^{\prime}\!\!{A^{\!\star}}={A^{\!\star}}-({\rm{diag}}(A^{\bar{0}})|O)
,

\pi_{r}\{\textsc{m}^{{}^{\prime}\!\!{A^{\!\star}},\bf 0}G_{\mathrm{co}(A)}\mid{A^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|\mathbb{N}_{2})_{r}\}=\{\mathbf{m}^{({A^{\!\star}})}\mid{A^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{N}|\mathbb{N}_{2})_{r}\}

forms a basis for
\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{\upsilon}(n,r)
. Hence,
\bm{\xi}_{n,r}
is surjective. The last two assertions follow easily.
∎

Remark 8.5
.

Using the formulas in Lemma
4.1
, Lemma
4.2
and Proposition
5.1
, it is straightforward to check that the ideal of
{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})
generated by the elements in (
3.3.1
)
is contained in
{\rm{ker}}(\bm{\xi}_{n,r})
. Then by the surjectivity of
\bm{\xi}_{n,r}
and a dimension comparison, one can deduce that
{\rm{ker}}(\bm{\xi}_{n,r})
actually coincides with
\rm{ker}(\Phi_{r})
in Proposition
3.3
. We may use the
\bm{{\mathcal{H}}}_{r}^{c}
-module isomorphism

\bm{V}(n|n)^{\otimes r}\cong\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Lambda(n,r)}x_{\lambda}\bm{{\mathcal{H}}}_{r}^{c}
to induce an isomorphism
\bm{\omega}_{r}:\bm{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{\upsilon}(n,r)\to\text{End}_{\bm{{\mathcal{H}}}_{r}^{c}}(\bm{V}(n|n)^{\otimes r})
. It is natural to expect that
\Phi_{r}=\bm{\omega}_{r}\circ\bm{\xi}_{n,r}
.

9.
Completing the proof of the main Theorem
1.4

In this section, we shall prove that
\bm{\xi}_{n}
in Theorem
8.3
(3) is injective and, hence, we obtain an isomorphism between
{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})
and
{\bm{\mathfrak{A}}_{v}(n)}
. Thus, the main theorem in subsection 1.4 follows.

In the quantum
\mathfrak{gl}_{n}
case, a monomial basis which is triangularly related to a PBW type basis is used to prove the type
A
map similar to
\bm{\xi}_{n}
is injective, see
[
BLM
, 5.7]
. Though this approach might still work in the queer case, we provide below a different approach.

We first introduce a PBW type basis for
{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})
. Following
[
Ol
, Th. 6.2]
and
[
DW1
, Rem.5.6, Lem. 5.7]
,
for
1\leq i\leq n-1
, set
6
6

6

Here
{{{\mathsf{E}}}}_{i,j}
is in fact the quantum root vector which is denoted by
{{X}}_{i,j}
in
[
DW1
]
.

\displaystyle{{{\mathsf{E}}}}_{i,i+1}={{\mathsf{E}}}_{i},\quad{{{\mathsf{E}}}}_{i+1,i}={{\mathsf{F}}}_{i},\quad{\overline{{{\mathsf{E}}}}}_{i,i+1}=\overline{{\mathsf{E}}}_{{i}}={{\mathsf{E}}}_{\overline{i}},\quad{\overline{{{\mathsf{E}}}}}_{i+1,i}=\overline{{\mathsf{F}}}_{{i}}={{\mathsf{F}}}_{\overline{i}},\quad\overline{\mathsf{K}}_{a}=\mathsf{K}_{\bar{a}}

for
|j-i|>1
and then introduce the following quantum root vectors:

\displaystyle{{{\mathsf{E}}}}_{i,j}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &{{{\mathsf{E}}}}_{i,k}{{{\mathsf{E}}}}_{k,j}-{v}{{{\mathsf{E}}}}_{k,j}{{{\mathsf{E}}}}_{i,k},&\mbox{ if }i<j,\\
&{{{\mathsf{E}}}}_{i,k}{{{\mathsf{E}}}}_{k,j}-{v}^{-1}{{{\mathsf{E}}}}_{k,j}{{{\mathsf{E}}}}_{i,k},&\mbox{ if }i>j,\end{aligned}\right.\quad{\overline{{{\mathsf{E}}}}}_{i,j}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &{{{\mathsf{E}}}}_{i,k}{\overline{{{\mathsf{E}}}}}_{k,j}-{v}{\overline{{{\mathsf{E}}}}}_{k,j}{{{\mathsf{E}}}}_{i,k},&\mbox{ if }i<j,\\
&{\overline{{{\mathsf{E}}}}}_{i,k}{{{{\mathsf{E}}}}}_{k,j}-{v}^{-1}{{{{\mathsf{E}}}}}_{k,j}{\overline{{{\mathsf{E}}}}}_{i,k},&\mbox{ if }i>j,\end{aligned}\right.

where
k
is any number strictly between
i
and
j
and it is known that
{{{\mathsf{E}}}}_{i,j}
and
{\overline{{{\mathsf{E}}}}}_{i,j}
does not depend on the choice of
k
.

Set

(9.0.1)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{G}}_{n}

\displaystyle=\{\mathsf{K}_{a},\mathsf{K}_{a}^{-1},\overline{\mathsf{K}}_{a},\mathsf{E}_{j},\mathsf{E}_{\bar{j}},\mathsf{F}_{j},\mathsf{F}_{\bar{j}}|1\leq a\leq n,1\leq j\leq n-1\},

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}_{n}

\displaystyle=\{\mathsf{K}_{a},\mathsf{K}_{a}^{-1},\overline{\mathsf{K}}_{a},\mathsf{E}_{i,j},\mathsf{E}_{j,i},\overline{\mathsf{E}}_{i,j},\overline{\mathsf{E}}_{j,i}|1\leq a\leq n,1\leq i<j\leq n\}.

Then
\widetilde{\mathscr{G}}_{n}\subseteq\mathscr{R}_{n}
and
\widetilde{\mathscr{G}}_{n}
is the inverse of (
8.1.1
) satisfying
\bm{\xi}_{n}(\widetilde{\mathscr{G}}_{n})=\mathscr{G}_{n}
.

For any
{A^{\!\star}}=(A^{\bar{0}}|A^{\bar{1}})=(a_{i,j}^{\bar{0}}|a_{i,j}^{\bar{1}})\in M_{n}^{\pm}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})
,
let

(9.0.2)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{B}}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bf{0}}={\prod_{{1}\leq j\leq{n-1}}}\ \Big({\prod_{{j+1}\leq i\leq n}}{{{\mathsf{E}}}}_{i,j}^{{{a}^{\bar{0}}_{i,j}}}{\overline{{{\mathsf{E}}}}}_{i,j}^{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}}\Big){\cdot\Big(\prod_{1\leq j\leq n}\overline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{j}^{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{j,j}}\Big)\cdot{\prod_{{n}\geq j\geq{1}}}\big({\prod_{{j-1}\geq i\geq{1}}}{{{\mathsf{E}}}}_{i,j}^{{{a}^{\bar{0}}_{i,j}}}{\overline{{{\mathsf{E}}}}}_{i,j}^{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}}\big)}.

By the matrix decomposition in (
7.12.2
), let

A^{\star}_{-}=(A^{\bar{0}}_{-}|A^{\bar{1}}_{-}),\quad A^{\star}_{0}=(O|A^{\bar{1}}_{0}),\quad A^{\star}_{+}=(A^{\bar{0}}_{+}|A^{\bar{1}}_{+}),

then we may rewrite

{\mathsf{B}}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bf{0}}={\mathsf{B}}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bf{0}}_{-}\cdot{\mathsf{B}}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bf{0}}_{0}\cdot{\mathsf{B}}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bf{0}}_{+}
, where
{\mathsf{B}}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bf{0}}_{\bullet}
is the product associated with
A^{\star}_{\bullet}
for all
\bullet\in\{-,0,+\}
.

For
\bm{j}=(j_{i})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
and
{A^{\!\star}}
as above, define
{\mathsf{B}}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bm{j}}=\prod_{i=1}^{n}{{\mathsf{K}}}_{i}^{j_{i}}\cdot{\mathsf{B}}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bf{0}}
.
These elements form a PBW type basis for
{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})
.

Proposition 9.1

(
[
Ol
]
,
[
DW1
, Prop. 5.8]
)
.

The set

\mathfrak{B}=\{\mathsf{B}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bm{j}}\ \bm{|}\ {A^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}^{\pm}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2}),\ \bm{j}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}\},

containing
\mathscr{R}_{n}
, forms a
\mathbb{Q}({v})
-basis for
{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})
.

Let
\bm{U}_{v}^{+}
(resp.
\bm{U}_{v}^{-}
) be the subalgebra of
{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})

generated by
\mathsf{E}_{j},\mathsf{E}_{\bar{j}}
(resp.
\mathsf{F}_{j},\mathsf{F}_{\bar{j}}
) with
1\leq j\leq n-1

and let
\bm{U}_{v}^{0}
be the subalgebra of
{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})
generated by
{{\mathsf{K}}}_{i}^{\pm},{{\mathsf{K}}}_{\bar{i}}
with
1\leq i\leq n
.
Then the proposition above implies that
{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})
admits the following triangular decomposition (see also
[
GJKK
, Th. 2.3]
):

(9.1.1)

{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})=\bm{U}_{v}^{-}\bm{U}_{v}^{0}\bm{U}_{v}^{+}\cong\bm{U}_{v}^{-}\otimes\bm{U}_{v}^{0}\otimes\bm{U}_{v}^{+},

and
\bm{U}_{v}^{-},\bm{U}^{0}_{v},\bm{U}_{v}^{+}
have bases, respectively,

(9.1.2)

\{\mathsf{B}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bf 0}\mid{A^{\!\star}}\in\Xi^{-}_{n}\},\quad\{\mathsf{B}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bm{j}}\mid{A^{\!\star}}\in\Xi^{0}_{n},\bm{j}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}\},\quad\{\mathsf{B}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bf 0}\mid{A^{\!\star}}\in\Xi_{n}^{+}\},

where
\Xi_{n}^{-}
(resp.,
\Xi_{n}^{0}
,
\Xi_{n}^{+}
) is the subset of
M_{n}^{\pm}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})
consisting of
{A^{\!\star}}=(A^{\bar{0}}|A^{\bar{1}})
such that both
A^{\bar{0}}
and
A^{\bar{1}}
are lower triangular (resp., diagonal, upper triangular) (cf. (
7.12.2
)).
Note that we have the decomposition

(9.1.3)

{M_{n}^{\pm}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})=\Xi_{n}^{-}+\Xi_{n}^{0}+\Xi_{n}^{+}.}

Let
\bm{U}^{0}_{\mathsf{K}}
(resp.,
\bm{U}^{0}_{\overline{\mathsf{K}}}
) be the subalgebra generated by
\mathsf{K}_{j}
(resp.,
\overline{\mathsf{K}}_{j}
) for all
j\in[1,n]
.
There is a
\bm{U}^{0}_{\mathsf{K}}
-module structure on
{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})
defined by conjugation action (see
[
GJKK
, p. 837]
):

\mathsf{K}_{i}*u:=\mathsf{K}_{i}u\mathsf{K}_{i}^{-1},\;\;\text{for all }u\in{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n}).

We now describe its weight subspaces.

Recall that the queer Lie superalgebra
\mathfrak{q}(n)
admits a Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}
(see
[
CW
, §1.2.6]
). Let
\mathfrak{h}_{\bar{0}}
be the even part of
\mathfrak{h}
with
h_{i},\;i\in[1,n],
as the standard basis.
7
7

7

Using the notation in (
5.0.1
),
h_{i}=D_{i}^{\star\!\!\!\!\square}
for
D_{i}=(E_{i,i}|O)
.
Let
\epsilon_{i}
be its dual basis defined by
\epsilon_{i}(h_{j})=\delta_{i,j}
and define roots

\alpha_{i,j}=\epsilon_{i}-\epsilon_{j}
for
1\leq i\neq j\leq n
. Then, we obtain the root lattice
\mathsf{Q}=\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{Z}\alpha_{j}
, where
\alpha_{j}=\alpha_{j,j+1}
.
Let
\mathsf{Q}^{+}=\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{Z}^{+}\alpha_{j}
and
\mathsf{Q}^{-}=-\mathsf{Q}^{+}
.

For any
\alpha\in\mathsf{Q}
, define the
\alpha
-
weight space

{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})_{\alpha}=\{u\in{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})\mid\mathsf{K}_{i}*u:=\mathsf{K}_{i}u\mathsf{K}_{i}^{-1}={\upsilon}^{\alpha(h_{i})}u\},

and write
\texttt{wt}(u)=\alpha
if
0\neq u\in{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})_{\alpha}
.
Then, by Definition
3.1
, we have

(9.1.4)

\mathsf{K}^{\pm}_{i},\mathsf{K}_{\bar{i}}\in{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})_{0},\quad\mathsf{E}_{j},\mathsf{E}_{\bar{j}}\in{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})_{\alpha_{j}},\quad\mathsf{F}_{j},\mathsf{F}_{\bar{j}}\in{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})_{-\alpha_{j}}\;\;(i\in[1,n],j\in[1,n)).

Thus, an induction on
|i-j|
shows that
\texttt{wt}(\mathsf{E}_{i,j})=\alpha_{i,j}=\texttt{wt}(\overline{\mathsf{E}}_{i,j})
. Hence, by (
9.0.2
),

(9.1.5)

\texttt{wt}({A^{\!\star}}):=\texttt{wt}(\mathsf{B}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bm{j}})=\texttt{wt}(\mathsf{B}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bf 0})=\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq n}a_{i,j}\alpha_{i,j},

where
A=(a_{i,j})
is the base of
{A^{\!\star}}
.
Hence, we have
{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\mathsf{Q}}{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})_{\alpha}
. Similarly, we have weight space decompositions

(9.1.6)

\bm{U}_{\upsilon}^{+}=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\mathsf{Q}^{+}}(\bm{U}_{\upsilon}^{+})_{\alpha}\;\text{ and }\;\bm{U}_{\upsilon}^{-}=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\mathsf{Q}^{-}}(\bm{U}_{\upsilon}^{-})_{\alpha}.

By (
9.1.2
), we have immediately the following

Corollary 9.2
.

For
\epsilon\in\{+,-\}
and
\alpha\in\mathsf{Q}^{\epsilon}
, each weight subspace
(\bm{U}_{\upsilon}^{\epsilon})_{\alpha}
has a basis

\{\mathsf{B}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bf 0}\mid{A^{\!\star}}\in\Xi^{\epsilon}_{n},\texttt{wt}({A^{\!\star}})=\alpha\}.

We are ready to introduce a monomial basis for
{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})
.
For any
{A^{\!\star}}=(a_{i,j}^{\bar{0}}|a_{i,j}^{\bar{1}})\in M_{n}^{\pm}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2})
with base
A=(a_{i,j})
and
\bm{j}=(j_{i})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, we use the same product order in (
8.2.5
) to define

(9.2.1)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{M}}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bm{j}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{n}{{\mathsf{K}}}_{i}^{j_{i}}\cdot{\prod_{{1}\leq j\leq{n-1}}}\Big({\prod_{j+1\leq i\leq n}}\mathsf{M}^{{A^{\!\star}}}_{i,j}\Big)\cdot\Big(\prod_{1\leq j\leq n}\mathsf{M}^{{A^{\!\star}}}_{j,j}\Big)\cdot{\prod_{{n}\geq j\geq{1}}}\Big({\prod_{{j-1}\geq i\geq{1}}}\mathsf{M}^{{A^{\!\star}}}_{i,j}\Big),\;\;\text{where}

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}^{A^{\!\star}}_{i,j}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}{\overline{\mathsf{E}}_{i}}^{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}}\cdot\mathsf{E}_{i}^{({a}^{\bar{0}}_{i,j})}\mathsf{E}_{i+1}^{(a_{i,j})}\cdots\mathsf{E}_{j-1}^{(a_{i,j})},\qquad&\mbox{for all }1\leq i<j\leq n,\\
\overline{\mathsf{K}}_{i}^{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,i}},\qquad&\mbox{for all }1\leq i\leq n,\\
{\overline{\mathsf{F}}_{i-1}}^{{a}^{\bar{1}}_{i,j}}\cdot\mathsf{F}_{i-1}^{({a}^{\bar{0}}_{i,j})}\mathsf{F}_{i-2}^{(a_{i,j})}\cdots\mathsf{F}_{j}^{(a_{i,j})},\qquad&\mbox{for all }1\leq j<i\leq n.\end{cases}

Theorem 9.3
.

The set

\mathfrak{M}=\{{\mathsf{M}}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bm{j}}\ \bm{|}\ {A^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}^{\pm}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2}),\ \bm{j}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}\}

forms a basis for
{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})
. In particular,
the superalgebra epimorphism
\bm{\xi}_{n}:{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})\to{\bm{\mathfrak{A}}_{v}(n)}
given in
Theorem
8.3
(3) is an isomorphism.

Proof.

By definition, we have

\bm{\xi}_{n}({\mathsf{M}}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bm{j}})={\textsc{m}}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bm{j}}
.
Thus, by Theorem
8.3
(2), the set
\{{\mathsf{M}}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bm{j}}\ \bm{|}\ {A^{\!\star}}\in M_{n}^{\pm}(\mathbb{N}|{{\mathbb{N}}}_{2}),\ \bm{j}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}\}
is linearly independent.

The monomial defined in (
9.2.1
) are also weight vectors. More precisely, by (
9.1.4
), we have the following weight formulas: for
1\leq i<j\leq n
,

{\texttt{wt}}(\mathsf{M}^{A^{\!\star}}_{i,j})=a_{i,j}^{\bar{1}}(\alpha_{i}+\alpha_{i+1}+\cdots+\alpha_{j-1})+a_{i,j}^{\bar{0}}(\alpha_{i}+\alpha_{i+1}+\cdots+\alpha_{j-1})=a_{i,j}\alpha_{i,j}.

Clearly, same formula holds for
i>j
. Thus, if
{A^{\!\star}}\in\Xi_{n}^{+}
, then

\texttt{wt}({\mathsf{M}}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bm{0}})=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}\texttt{wt}(\mathsf{M}^{A^{\!\star}}_{i,j})=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}a_{i,j}\alpha_{i,j}=\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}a_{i,j}\alpha_{i,j}=\texttt{wt}({A^{\!\star}}).

Hence,
(\bm{U}_{\upsilon}^{+})_{\alpha}
contains
\{\mathsf{M}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bf 0}\mid{A^{\!\star}}\in\Xi^{+}_{n},\texttt{wt}({A^{\!\star}})=\alpha\}
which is linearly independent. Consequently, a dimensional comparison with Corollary
9.2
, this set forms a basis for the weight space. This shows that the set

\{\mathsf{M}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bf 0}\mid{A^{\!\star}}\in\Xi^{+}_{n}\}
forms a second basis for
\bm{U}_{\upsilon}^{+}
.

Similarly, one proves that the set

\{\mathsf{M}^{{A^{\!\star}},\bf 0}\mid{A^{\!\star}}\in\Xi^{-}_{n}\}
forms a second basis for
\bm{U}_{\upsilon}^{-}
and clearly,

\bm{U}^{0}_{v}
has basis

\{\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathsf{K}_{i}^{j_{i}}\prod_{1\leq i\leq n}\overline{\mathsf{K}}_{i}^{t_{i}}\mid\bm{j}\in\mathbb{Z},\bm{t}\in\mathbb{N}_{2}^{n}\}.

Altogether, by (
9.1.1
), (
9.1.3
), and (
9.2.1
), we conclude that
\mathfrak{M}
forms a basis for
{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})
which forces the map
\bm{\xi}_{n}
is injective.
∎

Completing the proof of the main Theorem
1.4
.

By Theorems
8.3
and
9.3
, the map
\bm{\xi}_{n}:{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})\rightarrow{\bm{\mathfrak{A}}_{v}(n)}
is a superalgebra isomorphism. Thus, we identify the two superalgebras. In particular, under this identification,
{\bm{U}}_{\!{v}}(\mathfrak{{q}}_{n})
has three bases:

(1)

the PBW type basis
\mathfrak{B}
given in Proposition
9.1
;

(2)

the BLM type basis
\mathfrak{L}
given in Lemma
8.1
, and

(3)

the monomial basis
\mathfrak{M}
given in Theorem
9.3
.

So, the basis assertion follows.

Finally, the multiplication formula in (1) follows from Proposition
5.1
,
those in (2) and (3) follow from Proposition
5.2
, and the last one in (4) follows from Proposition
5.3
, the
h=n
case. ∎
